# Acceptance

Spec #1's Testing Decisions list the Gateway-seam acceptance scenarios, from plan Phases 2 to 4. Each row names the tests that prove the scenario. A test name is `Class.Method`. Classes without a project prefix are in `tests/ModularMonolith.Tests`, which drives the Proving Sample through Module Gateways (and HTTP) against PostgreSQL in Testcontainers. The `PouryaKit.Cqrs.Tests` classes run the dispatcher with stand-ins and no database, and support a row that a Gateway-seam test already proves. Every scenario has Gateway-seam proof; a row notes any supplementary check that calls the dispatcher directly.

The plan row is the B.1 behaviour the Phase acceptance line cites, or the Phase when it cites none.

The Wave 2 sections without a `validate.py` PASS line (among them #37, #38, #44, #56, #57 and the #54 packages), and the PASS lines older than their section's last source change, are covered by the integrated runs: the close-out's final `validate.py` run on the `feat/wave-2` tip, which #54's closing comment cites, is the package record for all of them.

| Scenario | Plan row | Proven by | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| A failed Result rolls back | 2 | `UnitOfWorkTests.A_failed_result_returned_after_writing_leaves_nothing_written`; `NotificationTests.An_operation_that_rolls_back_stores_no_notification` ("failed Result") | The second test also shows that no Outbox row is left. |
| Code that only reads cannot commit a retained tracked-state mutation | Phase 2 | `ReadOnlyStateTests.A_failed_or_throwing_query_cannot_mutate_a_held_or_newly_loaded_aggregate`; `ReadOnlyStateTests.A_caught_read_violation_still_rolls_the_outer_command_back`; `ReadOnlyStateTests.A_query_cannot_change_a_child_an_owned_value_or_pending_domain_events`; `ReadOnlyStateTests.Complex_collection_reads_compare_nested_values_and_collection_membership_on_postgresql`; `ReadOnlyStateTests.A_query_cannot_change_navigation_membership_when_automatic_change_detection_is_disabled` | Held and newly loaded values, nested queries, authorization, validation and explicit reading steps are covered. Companion tests preserve legitimate pending changes and query-only composition. Violations roll back even when caught, including navigation changes with automatic detection disabled. The complex-collection check uses the dispatcher and EF Unit of Work directly against PostgreSQL. |
| A command that raises no Domain Event is saved | 3 | `UnitOfWorkTests.A_command_that_raises_no_domain_event_is_committed_though_its_handler_never_saves` | |
| Chained Domain Events drain to a fixed point, and a cycle fails | 5 | `DomainEventTests.Domain_events_raised_by_handlers_are_handled_until_none_remain_up_to_the_depth_limit`; `DomainEventTests.A_cycle_of_domain_events_throws_at_the_depth_limit_and_leaves_nothing_written` | |
| Domain Event Handlers run one at a time | 6 | `DomainEventTests.The_handlers_of_a_domain_event_run_one_at_a_time_in_the_order_of_their_names` | |
| A nested failure (handler, validation, authorization, or inside a Domain Event Handler) rolls back data and Outbox | 12 | `UnitOfWorkTests.A_nested_command_that_fails_rolls_back_the_operations_data_and_outbox_though_the_outer_handler_ignores_it` (handler, validation, authorization: data and Outbox); `DomainEventTests.A_nested_command_that_fails_inside_a_handler_rolls_the_operation_back_though_the_handler_succeeds` (inside a Domain Event Handler: data and Outbox); `PouryaKit.Cqrs.Tests` `DispatchTests.A_nested_command_failure_the_outer_handler_ignores_still_rolls_the_operation_back` (also an exception, at the dispatcher) | In both Gateway-seam tests the Operation raises a Domain Event a Notification Handler subscribes to, so a commit would store a Notification; `UnitOfWorkTests.The_same_operation_with_a_nested_command_that_succeeds_commits_both_notes_and_stores_the_notification` shows that event stored when nothing fails, so the empty Outbox means something. |

## Structure seam

The third seam of the Testing Decisions is proven in `tests/PouryaKit.ArchitectureTests`, described in [the architecture doc](architecture/README.md#structure-tests). Each rule judges the Proving Sample and an offending fixture Module (`tests/PouryaKit.ArchitectureTests/Offending`) with the same function.

| Rule | Proven by |
|---|---|
| No Module references another Module's implementation; Contracts reference nothing from the Kit | `ModuleBoundaryTests` |
| A Module's only public types are its Composition Root and what its Host implements for it | `ModuleBoundaryTests` |
| Feature code never saves, commits, writes in bulk or calls a step only the Kit runs | `FeatureCodeTests` |
| No transport type in Feature code; no envelope storage in a Module's DbContext | `FeatureCodeTests` |
| No application code implements a contract the Kit fills for its pipeline, the dispatcher and the two optional slots included | `PipelineContractTests` (ticket #54) |
| Only the Kit's drain clears Domain Events | `FeatureCodeTests` |
| Only Domain sets up JSON options; Web, Cqrs and Caching apply `UsePouryaKitJson`, and Messaging.Wolverine alone its strict variant `UsePouryaKitWireJson` | `SharedJsonTests` (tickets #34, #60) |
| No Dapper write to an aggregate's table | `DapperWriteTests` |
| Only Core lists the types of an assembly; every other Kit package asks `ImplementingTypes` | `TypeLookupTests` (ticket #55) |
| Every Kit package's dependency closure matches plan section C | `PackageGraphTests` |
| No package with a licence outside the permissive list | `LicenceTests` |

## Stored-delivery schema regression

`StoredDeliveriesSchemaTests.Message_store_queries_use_the_exact_schema_name` exercises IDs, delivery enumeration, pending counts and deletion against PostgreSQL with `review-store` and `review-store"quoted`. These tests provision the message tables directly. They prove the reader quotes identifiers correctly; Wolverine 6.46.0 itself fails Host startup with these names in both Solo and Balanced mode, so this is not a Gateway or custom-schema Host-startup guarantee.

## Wave 2 shared JSON, ticket #34

Spec #33, user story 8: one JSON configuration for typed IDs, HTTP and message envelopes. `UsePouryaKitJson` in Domain is the configuration; Web and Messaging.Wolverine apply it.

| Acceptance | Proven by |
|---|---|
| A typed ID over Guid, long, int or string is written as its bare value and read back | `PouryaKit.Domain.Tests` `TypedIdTests.Ids_round_trip_through_json_as_their_bare_value`; `TypedIdTests.Ids_inside_an_object_are_written_as_their_bare_values_under_camel_case_names` |
| The configuration writes the same text from default options, Web-default options (applied twice) and options set to snake_case with the strict default encoder | `PouryaKit.Domain.Tests` `TypedIdTests.The_kits_json_options_write_the_same_text_from_default_web_or_snake_case_strict_options_and_when_applied_twice` |
| A typed ID has the same JSON in an HTTP response and in its delivered envelope, which reads back to the same ID | `SharedJsonTests.A_typed_id_is_written_the_same_in_an_http_response_and_in_its_delivered_envelope` |
| Guid typed IDs stay version 7 | `PouryaKit.Domain.Tests` `TypedIdTests.New_guid_ids_are_time_ordered` |
| One registration, in Domain; package references unchanged | `PouryaKit.ArchitectureTests` `SharedJsonTests`; `PackageGraphTests` |

The HTTP and envelope test compares `GET /catalog/products` (`ProductId` and `CategoryId`) with the stored envelopes of `ProductCreatedDomainEvent`, one per subscription, read from PostgreSQL in Testcontainers after delivery, and reads each back with the Host's own Wolverine serializer. With Wolverine's default options in place of `UsePouryaKitJson`, the envelope holds `{"value":"<guid>"}` and the test fails.

Packed API: the single-module clean consumer ([`scripts/packaging/consumers/single-module.cs`](../scripts/packaging/consumers/single-module.cs)) serializes a typed ID through `new JsonSerializerOptions().UsePouryaKitJson()` from the packed Domain. `python3 scripts/packaging/validate.py --no-fetch` on the fix-round source (commit `cf7d258`, before this paragraph was added) printed `PASS 13 packages, 15 clean consumers, negative controls and MinVer proof`, with the Release runtime suite at 391 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped.

## Phase 5 package proof, ticket #25

[`python3 scripts/packaging/validate.py`](../scripts/packaging/validate.py) runs the existing suite against one Release build, then packs with `--no-build`. Its evidence directory records runtime results separately from package and consumer results. [Package guidance](packages.md) gives the command, artifact contract and evidence limits.

| Acceptance | Proof |
|---|---|
| MinVer is shared, with minimum 0.1, v prefix and preview.0 default | Real package/build versions agree; `minver.json` records an isolated Git repository's untagged, tagged and post-tag outputs |
| Exactly the inventory's shipped packages (thirteen in Wave 1, fifteen with #37's Core and #38's Security, sixteen with #47's Observability, seventeen with #39's Security.JwtBearer, eighteen with #41's Web.OpenApi, nineteen with #51's Messaging.Wolverine.RabbitMq, twenty with #43's Caching); tests and samples excluded | Every project is evaluated against `inventory.json`; `InventoryTests.test_inventory_is_nonempty_and_exact` and `inventory-controls.log` reject empty/missing/extra/mismatched entries and accidental packability |
| MIT/notices, repository/source commit and SourceLink in each artifact | `PackageArtifactTests.Invalid_release_metadata_is_rejected`, `Embedded_source_link_identifies_the_commit_and_rejects_a_different_commit`, and the package validator inspect archive bytes and embedded PDBs |
| Existing graph and license restrictions survive shipping | `PackageGraphTests.Produced_nuspecs_obey_the_same_reference_rules_as_source_projects`, artifact graph checks and `consumer-closures.log` reuse `PackageGraph` and `LicenceTests` |
| Every package and supported composition restores/builds independently of Kit source | One package-only application per inventory package (fifteen since #38, sixteen since #47, seventeen since #39, eighteen since #41, nineteen since #51, twenty since #43), the single-Module set without Modularity/Messaging, and the copied modular monolith build with isolated caches, source mapping, exact versions and nupkg SHA-256 checks |
| Defects fail the shared path | Per-control logs show missing/extra artifacts, wrong version/commit, missing notices, forbidden references and invalid symbols rejected by the same package validator |

This is package restore/build proof, alongside the existing PostgreSQL runtime suite above. It proves neither hosted publication nor private-feed recovery.

## Phase 5 CI validation, ticket #26

[CI guidance](ci.md) defines the read-only reusable workflow, preserved artifact contract and trusted-main handoff. The shared path replaces the former source-only build/test job and retains the AI configuration check.

| Acceptance | Evidence and remaining gate |
|---|---|
| Build/test/pack/consumers share full-history Release output | Hosted [initial green](https://github.com/PouryaNoufallah96/PouryaKit/actions/runs/37448645751) and [repaired green](https://github.com/PouryaNoufallah96/PouryaKit/actions/runs/37449774763) ran the actual merge SHA with full history/tags and `--no-fetch`: 386 runtime tests passed, thirteen packages and fifteen clean consumers validated at MinVer `0.1.0-preview.0.8`. |
| Exact tested SHA and retained original artifacts | Downloaded artifacts `11404508475` and `11406765985` match all thirteen manifest checksums. Their receipts identify tested merge SHAs `b47372f2cc28feae58f2fb2f2f01f0cd98b825d8` and `165d1d82b559454bc2bc214d867aab72b4649ed4`, with distinct PR heads/base and bounded logs/assets without caches or configuration. |
| Failed validation stays failed and preserves diagnostics | [Run 37449281223](https://github.com/PouryaNoufallah96/PouryaKit/actions/runs/37449281223) tested merge SHA `49ff3f2cbd1900136745964bdeadaceb1aa496aa`: runtime passed, deliberate consumer `CS0103` failed the package step/job and whole CI. Artifact `11405776196` retains diagnostics and a failure receipt with no successful version/manifest pointer. |
| PR credentials and writes are isolated | All three runs' actual job setup logs show only `Contents: read` and `Metadata: read`; only AI setup and package validation ran. Checkout removed credentials; inspected YAML forwards no secrets. Main, tag/release census and fork settings compare unchanged. |
| Same-repository and fork execution | [Proof PR #30](https://github.com/PouryaNoufallah96/PouryaKit/pull/30) observed green/failure/repaired green and closed without merging. Real private fork execution remains unverified under the [owner exception](https://github.com/PouryaNoufallah96/PouryaKit/issues/26#issuecomment-6013184398); same-repository execution and static settings do not prove fork execution. |

The [CI evidence table](ci.md#hosted-proof-boundary) records all run identities and observed boundaries. Local script tests and workflow lint complement this hosted evidence. Publication remains outside this ticket and must verify a successful trusted main run at the exact selected SHA; a PR receipt alone cannot authorize it.

## Phase 5 private publication, ticket #27

The owner adjusted scope on 2026-10-06 to clean packaging, MinVer versioning and standard private NuGet publication. [Private publication guidance](private-packages.md) describes the active workflow; [ticket #27](https://github.com/PouryaNoufallah96/PouryaKit/issues/27) records actual hosted runs and consumer evidence.

| Active check | Evidence boundary |
|---|---|
| Exact successful trusted main run, attempt and SHA | The focused workflow check executes the real authorization script against accepted push/manual-main cases and rejected PR/fork/branch/SHA/attempt/failure/job cases. Actual run provenance is fetched read-only before the privileged job. |
| Original artifact, inventory and hashes | Official download uses the original run/attempt name and rejects digest mismatches. The focused check executes the artifact verification shell and rejects mismatched receipts, versions, missing inventory and changed package bytes. |
| One standard private publisher | Workflow lint and inspection check job-scoped package-write permission, the fixed feed and the literal publication group with cancellation disabled. Actual hosted serialization requires hosted evidence. |
| Private-feed availability | Successful [publication run 37466084084](https://github.com/PouryaNoufallah96/PouryaKit/actions/runs/37466084084) at main SHA `b234f4f` published the original `0.1.0-preview.0.13` packages and restored all thirteen with fresh caches and GitHub mapping. Its producer, [CI run 37465576160](https://github.com/PouryaNoufallah96/PouryaKit/actions/runs/37465576160), passed 386 runtime tests with zero failures or skips and validated thirteen packages and fifteen clean consumers. |
| Named preview/rc versions | Owner-created tags on current main followed by manual CI use MinVer's existing tag version through the same build/test/pack path. No automated tag, release or compatibility policy is claimed. |

The prior custom HTTP protocol, remote-byte verifier and preflight SDK machinery are removed. `--skip-duplicate` skips HTTP 409; it does not establish matching remote bytes. #28/#29 compatibility, automated named cuts and content-verified recovery are closed as not planned and require a new scoped ticket. The active Phase 5 scope is complete. The active pipeline does not satisfy all original #24 acceptance criteria. The successful [retry run 37465070135](https://github.com/PouryaNoufallah96/PouryaKit/actions/runs/37465070135) completed `.10` from original run `37456433718` and artifact `11408539884`, with Domain skipped on HTTP 409, twelve packages pushed and all thirteen restored with fresh caches. Preserve those original artifacts and the retry record; never replace or repack that version. This adds no remote raw-byte identity proof.

## Wave 2 Core helpers, ticket #37

[Ticket #37](https://github.com/PouryaNoufallah96/PouryaKit/issues/37) under spec #33. Core's helpers are proven at their public contract in `tests/PouryaKit.Core.Tests`, without a database, as spec #33's Testing Decisions place them; the cap is a Structure-seam test.

| Acceptance | Proven by | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Core supplies only predicate composition, implementing-type lookup and a synchronization-context scope, and a fourth public type fails a Structure test | `CoreTests.Core_exposes_its_three_helpers_and_no_other_public_type`; `CoreTests.A_fourth_public_type_in_core_is_rejected` | The negative case is `tests/PouryaKit.ArchitectureTests/Offending.Core`, Core's own sources plus a fourth public helper. `PackageGraph` gives Core no project and no package, and `NoPackages` keeps its closure empty. |
| Predicate composition uses AndAlso and OrElse and preserves short-circuit evaluation | `PredicateBuilderTests.AndAlso_is_true_when_both_are_and_evaluates_the_right_predicate_only_when_the_left_is_true`; `PredicateBuilderTests.OrElse_is_true_when_either_is_and_evaluates_the_right_predicate_only_when_the_left_is_false`; `PredicateBuilderTests.A_lambda_inside_the_right_predicate_that_binds_the_left_predicates_parameter_keeps_its_own_binding` | The right predicate counts its evaluations, so a bitwise composition fails both; each case compiles the composed lambda and checks its result for all four truth combinations. The third test is the review's regression: a hand-built right predicate whose nested lambda declares the left predicate's parameter instance. |
| Implementing-type lookup returns the implementations its documented input contract describes | `ImplementingTypesTests.An_open_generic_definition_finds_each_concrete_class_that_closes_it_directly_or_through_a_base_class`; `ImplementingTypesTests.A_non_generic_or_closed_contract_finds_each_concrete_class_assignable_to_it`; `ImplementingTypesTests.A_contract_that_is_neither_closed_nor_a_generic_definition_is_rejected` | Abstract classes, interfaces, structs and open generic classes are left out; an assembly given twice is read once. |
| The synchronization-context scope restores the prior context after normal and exceptional exit | `SynchronizationContextScopeTests.The_scope_sets_the_given_context_or_none_and_restores_the_prior_one_when_it_ends`; `SynchronizationContextScopeTests.The_scope_restores_the_prior_context_when_an_exception_ends_it`; `SynchronizationContextScopeTests.Disposing_a_scope_that_was_never_entered_changes_nothing` | Each test observes the context before, inside and after the scope, against a context it installed itself. |
| Package validation and clean consumers cover Core | `scripts/packaging/inventory.json` lists `PouryaKit.Core`; `PackageGraphTests.Every_kit_package_is_checked_and_closures_are_read_transitively` counts every Kit package (fourteen with Core, fifteen since #38, sixteen since #47, seventeen since #39, eighteen since #41, nineteen since #51, twenty since #43) | The shared validation path packs Core and builds a package-only consumer of it with the other packages. |

## Wave 2 unregistered permission declarations fail fast, ticket #56

Spec #33; owner decision of 2026-10-07 on the question #38 left open. No structure test: the check reads the handlers the Host registers, so a structure test over the Proving Sample would catch nothing it misses.

| Scenario | Proven by | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| A command or query that declares a permission whose check no Composition Root registered fails the Host when built, naming each such operation, its assembly and its Composition Root | `SecurityTests.A_command_or_query_that_declares_a_permission_whose_check_no_composition_root_registered_fails_the_host_when_built` (since #61 one of Cqrs's composition checks, still run when the container is built) | The Stub Module's command and query; goes red with the check switched off, and when any authorizer of the operation counts (the Stub's own authorizer of `StampNoteCommand` stays registered) |
| A permission declared in one Module and registered by another Module's Composition Root builds, and the check runs | `SecurityTests.A_permission_declared_in_one_module_and_registered_by_another_modules_composition_root_builds` | Registered in a second `AddPouryaKitModules` call, after the declaring Module |
| A Host whose Modules declare no permission builds and serves with no Security registration and no `IActorPermissions` | `SecurityTests.A_host_whose_modules_declare_no_permission_builds_and_serves_without_any_permission_registration` | |

## Wave 2 continuation trust only from the Kit, ticket #57

[Ticket #57](https://github.com/PouryaNoufallah96/PouryaKit/issues/57) under spec #33 (owner decision 2026-10-07; ADR 0007's amendment of that date). Proven at the Gateway seam, as permissions are in #38.

| Scenario | Proven by | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| A context that application code builds with any causation id is not trusted. A permission-protected command and query sent with it are refused `PermissionErrors.Denied` when the Actor lacks the permission, and nothing commits. | `SecurityTests.A_context_application_code_builds_with_a_causation_is_not_trusted_and_its_actor_is_checked` | The Stub Module's `StampNoteCommand` and `ReadStampLedgerQuery`. Goes red when the permission check reads `CausationId` again. |
| A context from `Continue()` on a context that never ran, or whose Operation has ended, is not trusted: a protected command and query sent with it are refused `PermissionErrors.Denied` | `SecurityTests.A_context_continued_outside_the_operation_that_ran_it_is_not_trusted` | Goes red when `Continue()` is always trusted, and when the check reads `CausationId` |
| `Continue()` inside a running authorized Operation is a trusted continuation (G.6). An Operation that declares no permission continues into a protected query, which its Actor holds no permission for, and the query is served. `Continue()` is not trusted inside the checks of a query nested in the Operation, nor from a task that outlives the Operation. | `SecurityTests.A_running_authorized_operation_continues_into_a_permission_protected_query_without_its_actor_being_checked_again`; `OperationContextTests.A_question_to_another_module_continues_the_asking_operations_context` | The Stub Module's `AskStampLedgerQuery`, and Catalog's product question as `CatalogProducts` asks it. Both go red when `Continue()` is never trusted. The first also goes red when the dispatcher no longer clears the context during nested checks, and when the context does not end with the Operation. |
| A delivery restored from the Kit's Outbox headers stays trusted, and an application authorizer still runs for it with the original Actor | `SecurityTests.A_continuation_runs_a_permission_protected_command_on_its_recorded_authority_without_authorizing_the_actor_again`; `SecurityTests.An_application_authorizer_still_runs_for_a_continued_command_with_the_original_actor_and_can_refuse_it` | From #38. The second test now asserts `IsTrustedContinuation`. Both go red when the delivery step restores the context with the public constructor. |
| Cqrs's `InternalsVisibleTo` lists exactly `PouryaKit.Messaging.Wolverine` and `PouryaKit.Web`, and neither calls an internal member of Cqrs other than `OperationContext.RestoreTrusted` | `TrustedRestoreTests.Cqrs_opens_its_internals_to_kit_delivery_and_web_only`; `TrustedRestoreTests.The_packages_cqrs_opens_to_use_no_internal_member_of_it_but_the_trusted_restore` | Structure test, read from the attribute list and the IL. It catches a third assembly being added to the list, and an opened package using another internal member, which no runtime test sees. There is no `Offending/` case: an assembly outside the list cannot compile a call to an internal member. The tests went red with `PouryaKit.Security` added to the list, and with Messaging.Wolverine reading `Operation.Context` (internal at the time; public since #47). |

## Wave 2 one implementing-type lookup, ticket #55

[Ticket #55](https://github.com/PouryaNoufallah96/PouryaKit/issues/55) under spec #33 (owner decision 2026-10-07: one implementation per concern). The six Kit registrations that found types themselves now ask `PouryaKit.Core`'s `ImplementingTypes`, and behaviour is unchanged: each registration's existing tests pass as they were.

| Acceptance | Proven by | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Cqrs, Security and Messaging.Wolverine reject what they rejected, with the same messages, from `ImplementingTypes.Candidates` | `PouryaKit.Cqrs.Tests` `RegistrationTests` (generic, unhandled, twice-handled, marker-authorized, multi-event and abstract-event declarations); `PouryaKit.Security.Tests` `RegistrationTests` (a permission on an abstract, generic or non-Operation type); `NotificationTests.Startup_fails_on_a_notification_handler_that_is_generic_or_handles_several_an_abstract_or_a_generic_domain_event` (Messaging.Wolverine's shape rejections, added in #55's fix round; red with `Of` in place of `Candidates`) | `NotificationTests.Startup_fails_on_a_missing_an_invalid_or_a_duplicate_name` proves only the naming rejections and their order: Cqrs and Messaging.Wolverine keep declaration order on top of `Candidates`, so authorizers run, and a clashing name is reported, as before |
| Web, Persistence.Dapper and Persistence.EFCore register what they registered, from `ImplementingTypes.Of` | `ModularMonolith.Tests` (every endpoint, Dapper read and typed-ID mapping of the Proving Sample) | Endpoints are mapped in full-name order instead of declaration order |
| `Candidates` returns every class the contract describes, abstract, open generic and compiler-generated included, as the old scans enumerated them, and leaves out interfaces and structs; `Of` still leaves abstract and open generic classes out | `ImplementingTypesTests.Candidates_include_the_abstract_and_open_generic_classes_that_of_leaves_out`; `ImplementingTypesTests.Candidates_include_a_class_the_compiler_generated`; `ImplementingTypesTests.An_open_generic_definition_finds_each_concrete_class_that_closes_it_directly_or_through_a_base_class` | The first fails with `Of`'s filter in `Candidates`, the second with a compiler-generated exclusion (#55's fix round). Core keeps three public types (`CoreTests`); the plan's I.3 row records the amendment |
| No Kit package but Core lists the types of an assembly | `TypeLookupTests.Only_core_lists_the_types_of_an_assembly`; `TypeLookupTests.Code_that_lists_the_types_of_an_assembly_itself_is_rejected` | Read from the IL: `Assembly.GetTypes`, `DefinedTypes`, `ExportedTypes`, `GetExportedTypes`, `Modules`, `GetModules`, `Module.GetTypes`, `Module.FindTypes`. Fails when `PouryaKitDbContext` calls `GetTypes()` again; the negative case is `Offending.OwnTypeLookup` |
| The six packages reference Core directly, as plan section C now allows | `PackageGraphTests` | Their rows in `PackageGraph`; Core's own row and `NoPackages` are unchanged |

`python3 scripts/packaging/validate.py --no-fetch` on the refactor's source (commit `d338d59`, before this paragraph was added) printed `PASS 15 packages, 17 clean consumers, negative controls and MinVer proof`, with the Release runtime suite at 423 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped. The package-only consumers of Cqrs, Web, Messaging.Wolverine, Persistence.Dapper, Persistence.EFCore and Security, and the copied Proving Sample, restored `PouryaKit.Core` transitively. A first run failed one test, `IntegrationEventTests.An_integration_event_whose_receiver_committed_on_a_host_that_died_before_acknowledging_it_is_applied_once`: the delivery was turned away twice instead of once while the effect stayed single, the Solo-mode recovery race between two live test Hosts that the [Testing section](architecture/README.md#pouryakittesting) describes; the rerun passed.

## Wave 2 PBKDF2 password hashing, ticket #40

Spec #33, user stories 22 and 23. Proven at the public contract in `tests/PouryaKit.Security.Tests` `PasswordHashingTests`, with no Host or database, as the spec's Testing Decisions place it; each test takes `IPasswordHasher` from `AddPouryaKitPasswordHashing`. The parameters, the format and their sources are in [security.md](../.agents/skills/pourya-kit/references/security.md#password-hashing).

| Scenario | Proven by | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| A hash verifies its password, and the default is PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 with 600,000 iterations, a 16-byte salt and a 32-byte key in the documented format | `PasswordHashingTests.The_default_hash_is_pbkdf2_hmac_sha256_with_600000_iterations_a_16_byte_salt_and_a_32_byte_key`; `PasswordHashingTests.A_hash_made_outside_the_kit_in_the_documented_format_verifies` | The key is checked against `Rfc2898DeriveBytes.Pbkdf2` called by the test, so the Kit adds no construction of its own |
| A wrong password fails | `PasswordHashingTests.A_wrong_password_fails` | Case, a trailing space and the empty password |
| Two hashes of one password have different salts | `PasswordHashingTests.Two_hashes_of_one_password_have_different_salts_and_both_verify` | Goes red with a fixed salt |
| A stored hash verifies with the parameters it carries; a weaker one needs an upgrade, an equal or stronger one does not | `PasswordHashingTests.A_hash_verifies_with_the_parameters_it_carries_and_needs_an_upgrade_only_when_weaker_than_configured`; `PasswordHashingTests.A_hash_with_fewer_iterations_than_options_now_allow_still_verifies_and_needs_an_upgrade` | More iterations, a longer salt, a longer key or another algorithm configured; a wrong password against each is `Failed`. Goes red with the upgrade signal off, and when verification uses the configured iterations |
| A malformed or unsupported encoding fails for the right password, without throwing | `PasswordHashingTests.A_malformed_or_unsupported_encoding_fails_for_the_right_password_without_throwing`; `PasswordHashingTests.A_missing_password_or_one_with_no_utf8_form_fails_verification_without_throwing` | 33 cases: prefix, field count, version, algorithm (SHA-1 and SHA-384 refused), iterations text (a sign, white space, a separator, a leading or trailing NUL, Arabic-Indic or full-width digits) and ceiling, non-canonical base64, salt and key length. A key cut to 31 bytes, or emptied, goes red without the minimum key length; the trailing NUL went red with `int.TryParse` alone (review fix F2) |
| Derived keys are compared in constant time | `PasswordHashingTests.Verification_compares_derived_keys_in_constant_time` | Reads the hasher's IL for `CryptographicOperations.FixedTimeEquals` and no `SequenceEqual`; goes red when the comparison is replaced |
| Options outside their bounds fail registration; options at their bounds are accepted | `PasswordHashingOptionsTests.Options_outside_their_bounds_fail_the_host_at_start`; `PasswordHashingOptionsTests.Options_at_their_bounds_let_the_host_start` | Iterations per algorithm and ceiling, salt and key size, an undefined algorithm. Since ticket #81 (2026-10-10) the options follow the options pattern and fail the Host's start instead of the registration; the two tests moved and were renamed ([its section](#pre-wave-3-public-api-changes-ticket-81)) |
| No new dependency | `PackageGraphTests` | #40 adds no project or package reference: Security's `PackageGraph` row and closure are unchanged by it, with no third-party package |
| The packed Security's hashing API works for a package-only consumer | Security's clean consumer, [`scripts/packaging/consumers/PouryaKit.Security.cs`](../scripts/packaging/consumers/PouryaKit.Security.cs) | It registers `AddPouryaKitPasswordHashing` and calls `Hash` and `Verify` against the packed package; `validate.py` builds `consumers/<name>.cs` for every consumer that has one. `python3 scripts/packaging/validate.py --no-fetch` on the fix-round source (commit `b4f3370`, before this row was added) printed `PASS 15 packages, 17 clean consumers, negative controls and MinVer proof`, with the Release runtime suite at 479 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped |

## Wave 2 Integration Event versioning, ticket #49

Spec #33, user stories 43 and 44. A wire name is one version of one contract type: Catalog publishes `ProductCreatedV1` (`catalog.product-created.v1`) and `ProductCreatedV2` (`catalog.product-created.v2`) from one Notification Handler during the transition; Inventory and Billing keep their v1 subscriptions, and Inventory's `inventory.product-categories` subscribes to v2 only. Nothing converts one version into another.

| Scenario | Proven by | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| One Operation publishes both versions, and each subscription applies only the version it declared, with its Receipt in the commit of its effect | `IntegrationEventVersionTests.One_operation_publishes_both_versions_and_each_subscription_applies_only_its_own_with_its_receipt_in_one_commit` | Against PostgreSQL: v1 envelopes only on `local://billing.product-created/` and `local://inventory.product-created/`, v2 only on `local://inventory.product-categories/`, no dead letter; one publication record per version; each effect carries the creating Actor, and its commit timestamp equals its subscription's Receipt's, both keyed by the one Domain Event identity. Goes red when every subscription is also routed the other version, and when Integration Event Receipts are keyed by Module instead of subscription. |
| A second type claiming a wire name, or one type under two wire names, is refused before the Host starts | `IntegrationEventWiringTests.A_composition_root_fails_on` ("a wire name twice", "a Domain Event's wire name", "one type under two wire names"); `IntegrationEventTests.Building_fails_when_another_host_of_the_process_already_sends_the_type_under_another_wire_name` | Refused by the declaration in the Composition Root, naming both types or both names; goes red with each check removed. |
| A type Wolverine would also route to another type's subscriptions fails the Host when it is built | `IntegrationEventVersionTests.Building_fails_when_wolverine_would_route_one_message_type_to_the_subscriptions_of_another` | Wolverine 6.46.0 routes a subscribed type to every message whose wire name, name or full name equals the type's full name, ignoring case. With the check removed, a v2 published under the v1 contract's full name was routed to `local://tests.routed/`, the v1 subscription's queue. |

Package proof: `python3 scripts/packaging/validate.py --no-fetch` on commit `d4eeb61` printed `PASS 15 packages, 17 clean consumers, negative controls and MinVer proof` at version `0.1.0-preview.0.29`, with its runtime suite at 421 tests, 0 failed, 0 skipped. Its copied Proving Sample consumer carries the v2 declarations. No public signature of `PouryaKit.Messaging.Wolverine` changed; only `Publish`'s documentation did.

## Wave 2 outgoing HTTP context and resilience, ticket #48

Spec #33, user stories 39 to 42; ADR 0007's amendment of 2026-10-07. The outgoing side runs against a WireMock.Net server (test-only), whose scenarios fail requests on cue and whose request journal shows what each attempt carried; its clients are composed with no Aspire package or ServiceDefaults. The receiving side runs on the Proving Sample's own Host, which reads its peers from configuration as the sample does (`PouryaKit:TrustedPeers:Peers`). Headers are written and read by `PouryaKit.Messaging.OperationContextHeaders`' names.

| Scenario | Proven by | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| A GET that fails transiently is retried to success, with identical Actor, tenant, correlation and causation headers on every attempt | `OutgoingHttpTests.A_safe_request_is_retried_through_transient_failures_with_the_same_context_on_every_attempt` | 503, 503, then 200; red when the causation changes per attempt or the handler writes nothing |
| Retries stop at the configured limit | `OutgoingHttpTests.Retries_stop_at_the_configured_limit` | `MaxRetryAttempts = 2` gives three attempts |
| Cancelling the call stops further attempts | `OutgoingHttpTests.Cancelling_the_call_stops_further_attempts` | One attempt arrives; without the cancellation, four do |
| A write is sent once by default and retried only by a client with the explicit replay-safe policy | `OutgoingHttpTests.An_unsafe_write_is_sent_once_by_default_and_retried_only_by_a_replay_safe_client` (POST and PROPPATCH); `OutgoingHttpTests.A_write_whose_connection_broke_is_not_sent_again_while_a_read_is` | A 503 and a reset connection; `HttpReplay.AllRequests` opts in. PROPPATCH goes red with an exclude-list of unsafe methods (`DisableForUnsafeHttpMethods`) in place of the allow-list |
| A client registered again keeps only the replay policy of its last registration | `OutgoingHttpTests.The_replay_policy_of_a_clients_last_registration_decides_whether_a_write_is_retried` | `RemoveAllResilienceHandlers()` between the two registrations, as with ServiceDefaults: `SafeMethods` then `AllRequests` retries a POST through a 503, the reverse sends it once. The first row was red before the re-review fix F9, when the allow-list stayed applied |
| A replay policy that is not defined fails the registration | `OutgoingHttpTests.A_replay_policy_that_is_not_defined_fails_the_registration` | `(HttpReplay)2` throws `ArgumentOutOfRangeException`; red when every value but `SafeMethods` enables write retries |
| An Actor id or tenant that is not a safe header value fails the call before anything is sent, so no header can be injected | `OutgoingHttpTests.A_context_value_that_is_not_a_safe_header_value_fails_the_call_before_it_is_sent` | CR LF and LF followed by `X-Injected: 1`, a character outside ASCII, an edge space, 257 characters; the request journal stays empty. Red without the check |
| A request without an Operation Context is refused before it is sent | `OutgoingHttpTests.A_request_without_an_operation_context_is_refused_before_it_is_sent` | Nothing reaches the server |
| An endpoint's outgoing call carries its own caller's context and never the caller's bearer token | `OutgoingHttpTests.An_endpoints_outgoing_call_carries_its_callers_context_and_never_the_callers_bearer_token` | Two callers at once through the Host's `downstream` client; red when the handler sets an `Authorization` header |
| A trusted authenticated peer continues the Operation its headers name; without them it runs as itself | `TrustedPeerTests.A_trusted_authenticated_peer_continues_the_operation_its_headers_name` | Red when the trusted branch is switched off |
| An untrusted person, an untrusted Host and an anonymous caller with the same headers keep their claims-derived identity and a new chain | `TrustedPeerTests.An_untrusted_or_anonymous_caller_with_the_same_headers_keeps_its_own_identity_and_a_new_chain` | Red when headers are bound for every caller |
| A trusted peer with incomplete or unreadable context headers is refused with a 400 problem response | `TrustedPeerTests.A_trusted_peer_whose_context_headers_are_incomplete_or_unreadable_is_refused_with_400` | Missing causation, a correlation that is not a Guid, an unknown Actor kind, a control character in the Actor id, a character outside ASCII in the tenant; the last two go red without the header rule |
| `PouryaKit.Web` references `Microsoft.Extensions.Http.Resilience`, and its closure holds no Aspire package | `PackageGraphTests` | Its row in `PackageGraph`; `A_closure_with_a_package_section_c_keeps_out_is_rejected` rejects an `Aspire.Hosting` arrival |
| The packed Web restores with its resilience closure and its outgoing API compiles in a clean consumer | `scripts/packaging/consumers/PouryaKit.Web.cs` | Registers a client with `AddPouryaKitResilience(...)` and `AddPouryaKitOperationContext()` and calls `WithOperationContext`, against the packed packages only |

`python3 scripts/packaging/validate.py --no-fetch` on the fix round's source (commit `732b97f`, before this paragraph was added) printed `PASS 15 packages, 17 clean consumers, negative controls and MinVer proof`, with the Release runtime suite at 505 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped. The package-only consumer of Web built from `consumers/PouryaKit.Web.cs` and restored `Microsoft.Extensions.Http.Resilience` 10.10.0 from the packed `PouryaKit.Web`.

## Wave 2 durable delayed continuations, ticket #44

Spec #33, user stories 19 and 35, and its acceptance "Persist a delayed ExpireQuote continuation". Billing's `OpenQuote` schedules `ExpireQuote` through `IDelayedCommands`; the failure cases use the Stub's `ScheduleNoteThenFail`. The recurring `ReconcileStock` declaration of the same acceptance belongs to #45.

| Scenario | Proven by | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| A delayed command is stored in the scheduling Operation's commit, due at its time, owned by no Host, survives the death of the Host that scheduled it, and runs once on a Host started afterwards, with the scheduling Actor, tenant, correlation and causation and its Receipt | `DelayedCommandTests.An_expiry_scheduled_by_a_host_that_dies_before_it_is_due_runs_once_after_a_host_starts_with_the_scheduling_context` | A database of its own, so only the Host started after the quick stop can run it. The envelope and the quote share one commit timestamp. Red when the Outbox drops delayed commands, when it writes them without `ScheduledTime` (stored `Incoming`, run at once), and when the delivery runs as a system Actor. |
| A delayed command handed out again after it ran finds its Receipt and repeats no effect | `DelayedCommandTests.A_delayed_command_handed_out_again_after_it_ran_finds_its_receipt_and_applies_nothing_more` | The row is set pending again, as a dead Host or a replay leaves it. Red without the Receipt: the expiry of an expired quote fails and is dead-lettered. |
| A scheduling Operation that fails, or whose nested command fails while it returns success, leaves no delayed command | `DelayedCommandTests.A_scheduling_operation_that_fails_or_whose_nested_command_fails_leaves_no_delayed_command` | Red when `Schedule` writes through Wolverine's message bus instead of the Unit of Work's Outbox. |
| A scheduling Operation whose save is refused after the Outbox step wrote its delayed command leaves none | `DelayedCommandTests.A_scheduling_operation_whose_save_is_refused_after_the_outbox_was_written_leaves_no_delayed_command` | A second `OpenQuote` of one quote fails on the primary key. Red when the Outbox writes delayed commands through Wolverine's message bus, outside the Unit of Work's transaction. |
| A delayed command stored under a name no longer declared is dead-lettered and never run | `DelayedCommandTests.A_delayed_command_stored_under_a_name_no_longer_declared_is_dead_lettered_and_never_run` | The stored row is moved to a retired queue before it is due. Red when the delivery finds its declaration by the command's type. |
| A delayed command not yet due is not pending for the test fixture, and the reset removes it | `DelayedCommandTests.A_delayed_command_not_yet_due_is_not_waited_for_and_the_reset_removes_it` | Due in an hour. Red, after 30 s, when a `Scheduled` row counts as pending whatever its time. |

## Wave 2 Operation observability and local delivery traces, ticket #47

Spec #33, its observability decision, and [ADR 0009](adr/0009-w3c-trace-context-through-the-outbox.md), item 3. The chain is Catalog's `CreateProduct`, then the delivery of its Notification `catalog.product-created.publish-product-created`, which publishes `ProductCreatedV1`, then Inventory's `inventory.product-created`, through durable local queues against PostgreSQL. The tests use listeners only, with no exporter or telemetry backend. The proof is local: cross-process propagation over RabbitMQ is #52's.

| Scenario | Proven by | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| A product created in Catalog and stocked in Inventory is one trace. `CreateProduct`'s Operation activity is a child of the upstream request, the publishing delivery's Operation a child of `CreateProduct`'s, and Inventory's delivery Operation a child of the publishing one: directly when only the Kit's source is listened, through Wolverine's execution span when `Wolverine` is sampled too. Each carries the upstream tracestate, and the product and its stock item are committed. | `ObservabilityTests.A_product_created_in_catalog_and_stocked_in_inventory_is_one_trace_with_the_upstream_tracestate` (both listener configurations) | Red when the Operation activity ends before the Unit of Work writes the Outbox, so the stored `parent-id` no longer names it. Red when the Outbox drops the tracestate header. Red, with only the Kit's source listened, when the delivery ignores the restored parent and every hop starts a new trace. |
| A stored retry, read back from the message store, stays in the publishing trace as a sibling of the failed attempt, with the tracestate, and the effect commits once | `ObservabilityTests.A_stored_retry_of_a_delivery_stays_in_the_publishing_trace_with_its_tracestate` | `DeliveryProbes.FailOnceAfterReceipt` fails the first attempt inside its transaction. Red when the header is dropped and when the fallback is removed. |
| Each command and query is one activity tagged with its Module, type, Actor kind and outcome (`success`, `failure` for a failed Result, `exception`), with its `ActivityStatusCode` and the Error codes or exception type. A nested command is a child of the one that sent it. Each has a duration measurement in fractional seconds with the same tags, an exception included. | `ObservabilityTests.Each_command_and_query_is_an_activity_and_a_fractional_duration_tagged_with_its_outcome` | The Stub Module's commands and query, through its Gateway. Red when the duration is recorded in whole seconds. |
| Observability references Cqrs only, and its closure holds no OpenTelemetry, Serilog, Wolverine or Messaging package | `PackageGraphTests` | Its row in `PackageGraph`. The plan listed Messaging too. Nothing from it is used, because a delivery records the parent it restored on Cqrs's `Operation`. |
| The packed Observability registers in a clean consumer, after the dispatcher it wraps | `scripts/packaging/consumers/PouryaKit.Observability.cs` | Calls `AddPouryaKitCqrs().AddPouryaKitObservability()` against the packed packages only |
| Packages | No third-party package added or bumped | `PouryaKit.Observability` is new, and is listed in `inventory.json` and in the modular-monolith composition |

`python3 scripts/packaging/validate.py --no-fetch` on the ticket's source (commit `49bc97e`, before this paragraph was added) printed `PASS 16 packages, 18 clean consumers, negative controls and MinVer proof` at version `0.1.0-preview.0.43`, with the Release runtime suite at 564 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped. The package-only consumer of Observability built from `consumers/PouryaKit.Observability.cs`, and the copied Proving Sample restored `PouryaKit.Observability` as a package.

The first four go red with the Kit's start check bypassed and signature, issuer, audience and lifetime validation off; "no subject" goes red when the normalization accepts a token without the Actor claim (Web would answer 500); "no audience" (review fix F2) goes red with `RequireAudience` off and its start check removed |

| A Host whose bearer options turn audience or issuer validation off, stop requiring the audience, name no audience or no issuer, have no signing key, skip lifetime validation, map inbound claims, set a validator delegate that replaces a required check, or would skip the normalization (events resolved by type, replaced in a later `PostConfigure`, or a `JwtBearerEvents` subclass) fails when it starts, never at the first request | `JwtBearerTests.A_host_whose_bearer_scheme_would_weaken_verification_fails_when_it_starts` | Seventeen cases, each `StartAsync` throwing `OptionsValidationException` with its one failure. ## Wave 2 durable recurring system work, ticket #45

Spec #33, user story 36, and its acceptance "Prove a recurring ReconcileStock declaration survives restart and runs under its named system Actor". Inventory declares `ReconcileStock` with `AddPouryaKitRecurringCommand` under the system Actor `inventory.stock-reconciler`; each test runs its Hosts on a database of its own at a cadence of one or three seconds, so the fixture's Host never runs an occurrence.

| Scenario | Proven by | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Distinct occurrences of `ReconcileStock` commit their reconciliations under the named system Actor, each a new Operation with a new correlation, no causation and no tenant, never a continuation; the occurrence a dying Host stored runs after the restart | `RecurringCommandTests.ReconcileStock_runs_under_its_system_actor_and_the_occurrence_a_dying_host_stored_runs_after_a_restart` | The Host is stopped while an occurrence is held after its commit, so the next occurrence is captured, by id and time, before the quick stop; after the restart that envelope is handled, and its reconciliation, committed after the death, and its Receipt exist. Three-second cadence. Red when the delivery continues the stored context, when no next occurrence is stored, when the next goes through Wolverine's message bus, when the Receipt is keyed by the envelope, and when no Host stores the first. |
| A starting Host stores the first occurrence only when none waits | `RecurringCommandTests.A_starting_host_stores_the_first_occurrence_only_when_none_waits` | Hourly, so nothing comes due: the first start stores the next whole hour, the second start the same one and nothing more. Red when a start always stores one, and when none does. |
| An occurrence delivered again finds its Receipt and neither reconciles again nor stores another occurrence, and the next occurrence is stored in the commit of the reconciliation | `RecurringCommandTests.An_occurrence_delivered_again_finds_its_receipt_and_neither_reconciles_again_nor_stores_another_occurrence` | The first occurrence is held after its commit to capture its envelope and the next one; the next is then held after its Receipt, so nothing else commits; the captured envelope is set pending again, as a dead Host or a replay leaves it. The stored occurrence and the reconciliation share one commit timestamp. Red when a delivery that finds its Receipt still stores a next occurrence, and when the next goes through the message bus. |
| A second chain, stored by a Host that starts while an occurrence runs, applies nothing at the first occurrence both chains reach, and ends | `RecurringCommandTests.A_second_chain_a_starting_host_stores_while_an_occurrence_runs_applies_nothing_and_ends` | The running occurrence is held after its Receipt, so nothing waits in the store when the second Host starts. Red when the Receipt is keyed by the stored envelope rather than the occurrence (an occurrence reconciled twice), and when a delivery that finds its Receipt stores a next occurrence. |
| A delayed command stored before its name was declared recurring is dead-lettered and never run | `RecurringCommandTests.A_delayed_command_stored_before_its_name_was_declared_recurring_is_dead_lettered_and_never_run` | A Stub command scheduled under `stub.kind-changed` by a Host that declares it delayed, then found by a Host that declares it recurring: `UnknownDelayedCommandException`, no note, no Receipt, no attempt. Red without the kind check. |
| A recurring command declared again with another occurrence factory is refused, naming the declaration it has | `RecurringCommandTests.A_recurring_command_declared_again_with_another_occurrence_factory_is_refused_naming_the_declaration_it_has` | Red with the earlier "declared under another name" message. |
| A Host whose recurring command has no cadence fails to start | `RecurringCommandTests.A_host_whose_recurring_command_has_no_cadence_fails_to_start` | `OptionsValidationException` naming the declaration. Red without the validation. |
| Fixture and sample | `NotificationTests.A_notification_committed_by_a_host_that_died_before_sending_it_is_handled_after_a_host_starts`; `PersistenceTests.Each_module_has_its_own_schema_and_migrations_history_with_snake_case_names`; `DapperWriteTests.No_module_writes_an_aggregate_table_with_sql_and_inventorys_snapshot_copy_is_seen_as_a_write` | A Host started on the fixture's database stores its first occurrence too, so the notification test reads its own queue; Inventory's schema gains `stock_reconciliations`, written with plain SQL and mapped by no aggregate. |
| Packages | No package added | `PouryaKit.Messaging.Wolverine` gains public `AddPouryaKitRecurringCommand`, `PouryaKitRecurringCommandOptions`, `DelayedCommandHeaders` and `IDelayedCommandOutbox.AddOccurrence`; no reference added. The new clean consumer `scripts/packaging/consumers/PouryaKit.Messaging.Wolverine.cs` declares a recurring command and its cadence against the packed package only, and the `modular-monolith` composition builds Inventory's declaration from packages. |

## Wave 2 transactional request idempotency, ticket #42

Spec #33, user stories 24 to 26, its idempotency decision, and the [ADR 0002](adr/0002-no-mediator-dependency.md) amendment for the first optional pipeline slot. The Stub Module's `CountNoteCommand` writes a note, stores a Notification and answers a value no other run answers, so an unguarded repeat would visibly succeed again; `PostNoteThenNestCommand` is the shape of a command without a value. The Proving Sample opts in `CreateProduct`, over HTTP, and `ImportProducts`.

| Scenario | Proven by | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| A repeat with the same request id replays the first success, an equal non-default value, without running the handler, writing a row or storing a delivery. The value is recorded in the Kit's JSON representation, and the Notification of the first run is delivered with no request id | `IdempotencyTests.A_repeated_request_id_replays_the_first_success_without_running_writing_or_sending_anything` | Red when the key is reserved after the handler, when the success is not recorded (the replay then throws rather than invent one), when the replay returns a default value, and when the value is written without `UsePouryaKitJson`. |
| A failed nested command leaves no key, so a later attempt with the same request id runs; its success, of a command without a value, then replays without running | `IdempotencyTests.A_failed_nested_command_leaves_no_key_so_a_later_attempt_runs_and_its_success_then_replays` | Red when the key is inserted on a connection of its own, outside the Unit of Work's transaction: the defect of both reference projects. |
| The key separates Actors, by kind and id, tenants and commands; a null tenant keeps one stable key, stored as the empty string | `IdempotencyTests.The_key_separates_actors_tenants_and_commands_and_a_null_tenant_keeps_one_stable_key` | Red when the tenant, the Actor's kind or the command is left out of the key, and when the null tenant's sentinel is not stable. |
| No tenant can equal that sentinel: a blank tenant is refused, and a blank tenant claim over HTTP is no tenant, so its request and one without the claim are one caller's, with one key | `PouryaKit.Cqrs.Tests.IdempotencyTests.A_blank_tenant_is_refused_so_no_tenant_shares_the_key_of_an_operation_without_one` (empty, space); `IdempotencyTests.A_blank_tenant_claim_runs_without_a_tenant_and_shares_the_key_of_a_caller_without_one` | Review fix F1. Red when `OperationContext` accepts a blank tenant, and when the HTTP boundary passes a blank claim on (the request then fails with 500). `TestAuthentication.BlankTenant` issues the empty claim. |
| An OpenAPI operation whose endpoint requires the header declares it as a required string header parameter, with the header rule, in every version; no other operation does | `OpenApiTests.An_operation_whose_endpoint_requires_an_idempotency_key_declares_the_required_header_in_every_version` | Review fix F3: `POST /catalog/products` on both paths of v1 and v2, not `POST /catalog/categories`. Red without the transformer. |
| A Module that reserves a key or records a success itself is rejected | `PouryaKit.ArchitectureTests.FeatureCodeTests.A_module_that_saves_commits_writes_in_bulk_or_runs_a_kit_step_is_rejected` | Review fix F2: `IIdempotencyStore` is one of `ModuleRules.KitSteps`, with offending calls in the `Offending` module. ## Wave 2 RabbitMQ transport, ticket #51

Spec #33, user story 45, its RabbitMQ decision and Testing Decision "Run real RabbitMQ and PostgreSQL with separate producer/consumer Hosts", on the topology of ADR 0006's #50 amendment, each row of its invariants table. `RabbitMqDeliveryTests` runs the Proving Sample's service composition: `ModularMonolith.Host` with the connection string `broker`, which hosts Catalog and Billing under the service name `modular-monolith`, and `Inventory.Host`, the unchanged Inventory Module under `inventory`, plus the tests' own second subscription `inventory.audit-product` to `ProductCreatedV1` and `DeliveryProbes`. Each is a real `IHost` from the sample's own composition, on a database of its own, joined only by RabbitMQ 4.3.6 (`Testcontainers.RabbitMq`, a vhost per test) and PostgreSQL 18.6 in Testcontainers. The tests live in `tests/ModularMonolith.Tests`, beside `AppFixture` but with their own `BrokerFixture`, because Catalog's commands are visible to that project only and the tests reuse its `DeliveryProbes`, `TestIssuer` and `AppFixture.ComposeHost`. Each "red" note names the behaviour switched off in a scratch run that failed the test.

| Scenario | Proven by | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| A publication whose only subscription is in another Host is written to the Outbox in the commit that published it, and applied there once, its effect and Receipt in one commit; the publishing Host never receives its own publications back from the broker | `RabbitMqDeliveryTests.A_publication_whose_only_subscription_is_in_another_host_is_written_in_its_commit_and_applied_there_once` | `ProductCreatedV2`, whose only subscription is in `Inventory.Host`: held after the commit of the Notification Handler's delivery that published it, its outgoing row to `rabbitmq://exchange/catalog.product-created.v2` has the commit timestamp of the publication record. Red without the exchange route from the publication, and with the Host's queue bound to the wire names it publishes. |
| Two subscriptions of the receiving Host to one wire name: the failing one, whose handler writes its effect and returns a failed Result, is retried with the stored retry, leaves neither its effect nor its Receipt, is dead-lettered at `local://{subscription}/`, and once healed its replay commits one effect with its Receipt; the other commits its effect and Receipt once, without waiting for it | `RabbitMqDeliveryTests.A_failing_subscription_in_the_other_host_rolls_back_its_effect_is_dead_lettered_alone_and_its_replay_applies_once` | The tests' audit subscription writes `tests.product_audits` in Inventory's Unit of Work; it is held after its Receipt while the stock item commits, then fails three times with `IntegrationEventHandlerFailedException` (review finding F3). Red with the fan-out replaced by delivering every subscription in-line in the broker envelope, and with a failed delivery's transaction committed instead of rolled back. |
| A Host killed between storing the copies and marking the broker envelope handled fans it out again after it restarts, and each effect is applied once | `RabbitMqDeliveryTests.A_host_killed_between_storing_the_copies_and_marking_the_broker_envelope_handled_fans_out_again_and_applies_each_effect_once` | A trigger makes the broker rows' handled mark wait on an advisory lock the test holds; all four copies commit while the three broker rows are not handled; the Host is stopped as a kill leaves it (`StopMode.Quick`), the waiting backend terminated, and a new Host started: three broker rows handled, eight copies, one Receipt and effect each. This tests the order ADR 0006's #50 amendment read but did not test: Wolverine stores the copies before it marks the envelope handled. Red with the Receipt's result ignored. |
| A copy the receiving Host's store refuses fails its broker envelope, which is never marked handled: it is retried and dead-lettered in the store at `rabbitmq://queue/inventory`, and once the store is healed its replay fans it out again and each subscription applies it once with its Receipt | `RabbitMqDeliveryTests.A_copy_the_store_refuses_leaves_the_broker_envelope_unhandled_dead_lettered_at_the_host_queue_and_its_replay_applies_each_effect_once` | Review finding F6. A trigger on `wolverine.wolverine_incoming_envelopes` makes PostgreSQL refuse every insert of the audit's copy: while it stands, no audit copy and no audit Receipt, and the broker envelope is a dead letter whose exception is `Npgsql.PostgresException`; once the trigger is dropped and the dead letter replayed, one handled audit copy, one audit with its Receipt and one stock item. Red with the copies sent from the handler's own context, whose flush drops a copy the store refuses and marks the broker envelope handled. |
| A copy its Host was delivering when it was killed is handed out again after the restart and applied once with its Receipt; the envelope is not fanned out again | `RabbitMqDeliveryTests.A_copy_its_host_was_delivering_when_it_was_killed_is_delivered_after_it_restarts` | The audit's copy is held after its Receipt once the broker envelope is marked handled, the held transaction ended and the Host stopped as a kill leaves it. The copy is sent with `EndpointFor` through a bus enlisted in no transaction, and its durable local queue stores it as an incoming delivery owned by the node before the fan-out returns. Red when local queues keep what they receive in memory only. |
| A publication that comes back to the publishing Host through a binding the Kit never makes reaches its subscriptions once more, where their Receipts stop it, and never goes round the broker again | `RabbitMqDeliveryTests.A_publication_that_comes_back_through_a_binding_the_kit_never_makes_is_applied_once_and_never_sent_round_again` | The queue `modular-monolith` is bound by hand to `catalog.product-created.v1`, as an earlier deployment that received it would leave it: one broker envelope, handled, still one a second later, an empty queue, Billing turned away once and one price list (review finding F2). Red with the copies published through the type's routes, which include its exchange. |
| A retry waiting in the receiving Host survives its kill and restart, and a publication made while it is down waits in its queue and is delivered after it starts | `RabbitMqDeliveryTests.A_retry_waiting_in_the_other_host_survives_its_kill_and_a_publication_made_while_it_is_down_arrives_after_it_starts` | The waiting audit is a `Scheduled` row at `local://inventory.audit-product/`; three messages wait in the queue `inventory`. Red with the Host's queue declared auto-delete, and with an in-memory retry. |
| Two Hosts with different service names that subscribe to one wire name each receive every message | `RabbitMqDeliveryTests.Two_hosts_with_different_service_names_each_receive_every_message` | `inventory` and `warehouse`, each with its own database, three products. Red with one queue name for every Host. |
| The store keeps `IdOnly`, the broker envelope and each copy have an id of their own, both Receipts carry the Domain Event's identity, and the remote delivery continues the publishing Operation with its Actor, tenant and correlation, the publishing delivery as causation, as a trusted continuation | `RabbitMqDeliveryTests.Each_local_copy_carries_the_broker_envelopes_identity_and_operation_context_under_an_id_of_its_own` | Red with the tenant header left out of the fan-out. |
| Each version crosses the broker under its own wire name and reaches only its own subscriptions | `RabbitMqDeliveryTests.Each_version_crosses_the_broker_under_its_own_wire_name_and_reaches_only_its_own_subscriptions` | One broker envelope per wire name, each copy only on the queues of its version's subscriptions, no dead letter. Red with the fan-out copying to every subscription's queue whatever its type. |
| Snapshots handed out again across the broker in reverse order, or two versions concurrently, leave the newest; a live Snapshot after the Tombstone does not bring the copy back | `RabbitMqDeliveryTests.Snapshots_redelivered_across_the_broker_in_reverse_order_leave_the_newest`; `RabbitMqDeliveryTests.Two_snapshot_versions_redelivered_across_the_broker_concurrently_leave_the_newer` (two rows); `RabbitMqDeliveryTests.A_live_snapshot_redelivered_across_the_broker_after_the_tombstone_does_not_bring_the_copy_back` | The broker envelopes are handed out again and fanned out once more, as `SnapshotTests` does with local ones. Red with the version gate accepting every version (the row `newerFirst: False` is the symmetric case and stays green by design), and the reverse-order test also with a received Snapshot delivered as an Integration Event. |
| A message the Host cannot fan out is dead-lettered in its store, received at `rabbitmq://queue/inventory`, never on the broker | `RabbitMqDeliveryTests.A_message_the_host_cannot_fan_out_is_dead_lettered_in_its_store_at_its_broker_queue` | A wire name the Host does not receive, published straight to its queue. Red without `DisableDeadLetterQueueing()`: the Host then refuses to start. |
| A Host whose broker listener does not store what it receives, or sets dead letters aside on the broker, does not start | `RabbitMqDeliveryTests.A_host_whose_broker_queue_does_not_store_what_it_receives_or_sets_dead_letters_aside_on_the_broker_does_not_start` (two rows) | `BufferedInMemory()` and `DeadLetterQueueing(...)` on the Host's queue. Red without the check. |
| `Inventory.Host` run as its own OS process applies a product Catalog creates, each effect with its Receipt, by the Actor who created it, from a copy that carries the publishing Operation's tenant, correlation and causation | `RabbitMqDeliveryTests.A_product_catalog_creates_is_applied_by_inventory_host_running_as_its_own_process_with_the_operation_context` | `dotnet run --no-build --project samples/ModularMonolith/src/Inventory.Host` in the tests' build configuration, configured through its environment, as a deployment configures it; the test waits until it consumes from its queue. The in-process rows above prove the context the delivery runs with. Red without the exchange route from the publication. |
| A message crosses the broker in the Kit's JSON: a typed ID as its bare value, read back as the typed ID | `RabbitMqDeliveryTests.A_typed_id_crosses_the_broker_as_its_bare_value_and_is_read_back_as_the_typed_id` | Contracts carry no typed ID, so a test-only record is published straight through Wolverine with the Kit's headers. `WolverineFx.RabbitMQ` brings Newtonsoft.Json along; the stored broker envelope is `application/json` with `"probeId":"<guid>"`. Red without the Kit's JSON options on Wolverine. |
| `PouryaKit.Testing` names a delivery or dead letter received at a broker queue, before its fan-out, by that queue's address | `StoredDeliveriesSchemaTests.A_delivery_received_at_a_broker_queue_before_it_reached_a_subscription_is_named_by_that_address` | `HostFixture.DeadLettersAsync` uses the same `StoredDeliveries.SubscriptionAt`. Red with the name read from the address's host, `queue`. |

## Wave 2 query caching and commit-aware invalidation, ticket #43

Spec #33, user stories 29 to 34, its caching decision, and the [ADR 0002](adr/0002-no-mediator-dependency.md) amendment for the second optional pipeline slot. The Proving Sample caches Catalog's `GetProduct`, which `RenameProduct` and `RemoveProducts` invalidate. A product renamed with plain SQL behind the cache's back tells a stored answer, the old name, from a read of the database, the new one; the Stub Module's cacheable queries record each run of their handler.

| Scenario | Proven by | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| An entry answers only the Actor and tenant that stored it: another person, a system Actor with the same id, and two tenants of one Actor each read their own | `CachingTests.An_entry_answers_only_its_own_actor_and_tenant` | Red when the Actor's id, its kind or the tenant is left out of the key. |
| A failed Result is never stored: a product created after a `NotFound` is found by the next query | `CachingTests.A_failed_result_is_never_stored` | Red when the failure is stored and returned on the next read. |
| Two concurrent identical queries in one Host run the handler once: the second joins the held first run, seen as `HybridCache`'s `StampedeJoin` event | `CachingTests.Two_concurrent_identical_queries_in_one_host_run_the_handler_once` | Red with a get-then-set in place of one `GetOrCreateAsync`. Single flight across Hosts is not claimed. |
| A committed rename evicts every Actor's warm entry, and a warm read answers the new name | `CachingTests.A_committed_rename_evicts_every_actors_entry_and_a_warm_read_answers_the_new_name` | Red when the dispatcher evicts nothing and when `RenameProduct` declares no tag. |
| After a committed removal, a warm read is `NotFound` for every Actor | `CachingTests.After_a_committed_removal_a_warm_read_is_not_found_for_every_actor` | `RemoveProducts` removes by name, so it declares `products`, a tag every `GetProduct` entry carries. Red when it declares no tag. |
| A rolled-back rename, refused at the save with `NameInUse`, evicts nothing: the valid entry survives | `CachingTests.A_rolled_back_rename_evicts_nothing_so_the_valid_entry_survives` | Red when the tags are evicted whatever the outcome. |
| A query nested in a write Unit of Work that rolls back leaves no entry: it saw the uncommitted tally inside, and runs again outside | `CachingTests.A_query_nested_in_a_write_unit_of_work_that_rolls_back_leaves_no_entry` | Red when a query inside an active Unit of Work goes through the cache. |
| A cached query's handler runs inside its Operation, so a continuation it creates is trusted | `CachingTests.A_cached_querys_handler_runs_inside_its_operation_so_a_continuation_it_creates_is_trusted` | Red when the slot passes the caller's token to `GetOrCreateAsync`, which makes `HybridCache` run the handler on the thread pool without the caller's `ExecutionContext`. |
| A caller that leaves a run it started, its token cancelled while the handler is held, keeps its scope and Operation alive: the joiner receives the non-default tally and a trusted continuation, read through the Module's DbContext, from one run (review finding F1) | `CachingTests.A_caller_that_leaves_the_run_it_started_keeps_its_operation_so_a_joiner_gets_a_trusted_success_of_one_run` | Red, with `ObjectDisposedException` from the disposed DbContext, when the slot passes the caller's token. |
| A query that changes tracked state and returns a success stores nothing: the starting caller and its joiner both receive the read-only check's `InvalidOperationException`, and a later read runs the handler again (review finding F2) | `CachingTests.A_query_that_changes_tracked_state_stores_nothing_and_its_joiner_receives_the_failure` | Red, with the joiner served the changed value, when the reading boundary closes only after the answer is stored. |
| A query whose answer changes tracked state when serialized, a deferred enumerable that renames the folder it read, stores nothing: the starting caller and its joiner both receive the read-only check's `InvalidOperationException`, and a later read runs the handler again (re-review finding F6) | `CachingTests.A_query_whose_answer_changes_tracked_state_when_serialized_stores_nothing_and_its_joiner_receives_the_failure` | Red, with the success stored and no exception, when the read ends before the answer is serialized. |
| A cacheable query nested in an uncached query that relabelled the folder stores nothing: the Operation fails on the outer query's read-only check, and a later Operation reads the committed colour (re-review finding F5) | `CachingTests.A_query_nested_in_one_that_changed_tracked_state_stores_nothing_so_a_later_read_answers_the_committed_value` | Red, with the uncommitted colour served from the cache, when the slot takes every query outside a write Unit of Work, as before the fix. |
| A cacheable query an authorizer sends after relabelling the folder stores nothing: the command fails on its checks' read-only check before its Unit of Work starts, and a later Operation reads the committed colour (re-review finding F5) | `CachingTests.A_query_sent_by_an_authorizer_that_changed_tracked_state_stores_nothing_so_a_later_read_answers_the_committed_value` | Red, with the uncommitted colour served from the cache, under the same switch. |
| A cacheable query nested in another query is answered but never cached: the next outermost read runs the handler again, and the one after it is served from the cache | `CachingTests.A_query_nested_in_another_is_answered_but_never_cached` | Red, with one run, under the same switch. Nested reads not being cached is the documented trade-off. |
| A query in an Operation that has already failed stores nothing: after a refused command in the same scope, the query answers the colour its Operation still tracks, and a later Operation reads the committed one (re-review finding F5) | `CachingTests.A_query_in_an_operation_that_already_failed_stores_nothing_so_a_later_read_answers_the_committed_value` | Red, with the rolled-back colour served from the cache, when the slot does not refuse a rollback-only Operation. |
| A committed rename stands, and this Host's entry is still evicted, when the distributed cache refuses the eviction; the refusal is logged | `CachingTests.A_commit_stands_and_evicts_this_hosts_entry_when_the_distributed_cache_refuses_the_eviction` | Red, with the command throwing after its commit, when the refusal is not caught. |
| With Redis, a second Host reads the entry the first stored without running its handler, and another Actor's read there still misses | `CachingTests.With_redis_a_second_host_reads_the_entry_the_first_stored_without_running_its_handler` | `Testcontainers.Redis`, `redis:8.10.1`; both Hosts register `AddStackExchangeRedisCache` from `ConnectionStrings:cache`, as the sample Host does. Red when the Host registers no Redis. Eviction of another Host's in-memory copies is not claimed. |
| A query reaches the cache after its authorization and validation, never inside a write Unit of Work, and never from a command's checks, the outermost command's included, which run before its Unit of Work starts | `PouryaKit.Cqrs.Tests.QueryCacheTests.A_query_reaches_the_cache_after_its_checks_and_never_from_a_command_or_its_checks` | Red when the lookup runs before the checks, and when the slot takes every query outside a write Unit of Work, as before re-review finding F5. |
| The tags of the outermost and nested commands are evicted after the commit, and never after a rollback | `PouryaKit.Cqrs.Tests.QueryCacheTests.The_tags_of_the_outermost_and_nested_commands_are_evicted_after_the_commit_and_never_after_a_rollback` | Red when a nested command's tags are not collected and when a rollback evicts. |
| Feature code never calls the query cache, a step only the Kit runs | `PouryaKit.ArchitectureTests.FeatureCodeTests.A_module_that_saves_commits_writes_in_bulk_or_runs_a_kit_step_is_rejected` | `Offending` calls `IQueryCache.EvictAsync`. |
| `PouryaKit.Caching` references Cqrs and `HybridCache` only, and no Redis | `PouryaKit.ArchitectureTests.PackageGraphTests` | Its section C row, and `StackExchange.Redis` kept out of its closure, with a negative case. |
| One JSON representation: Caching applies `UsePouryaKitJson` to the options it stores an answer with | `PouryaKit.ArchitectureTests.SharedJsonTests.Web_messaging_wolverine_cqrs_and_caching_apply_domains_json_options_and_nothing_else_sets_up_its_own` | |
| The packed API compiles in a clean consumer | `scripts/packaging/consumers/PouryaKit.Caching.cs` (new) | Registers `AddPouryaKitCaching` after `AddPouryaKitCqrs`, with a cacheable query and an invalidating command. The `modular-monolith` composition builds the sample's declarations from packages. |
