The clash check of D16 ran the same kind of metadata reader over every assembly of the restored WolverineFx 6.48.2 packages, `<NuGet global packages folder>/wolverinefx*/6.48.2/lib/net10.0/*.dll`, listing each exported type named `IOutbox` or ending in `Outbox`:

```text
Wolverine.EntityFrameworkCore.dll (57 exported types): Wolverine.EntityFrameworkCore.DbContextOutbox, Wolverine.EntityFrameworkCore.DbContextOutbox`1, Wolverine.EntityFrameworkCore.IDbContextOutbox, Wolverine.EntityFrameworkCore.IDbContextOutbox`1
Wolverine.Newtonsoft.dll (2 exported types): none
Wolverine.Postgresql.dll (24 exported types): none
Wolverine.RabbitMQ.dll (39 exported types): none
Wolverine.RDBMS.dll (49 exported types): none
Wolverine.RuntimeCompilation.dll (1 exported types): none
Wolverine.dll (744 exported types): Wolverine.Persistence.Durability.IMessageOutbox
```

The pin is 6.48.3 since #75's follow-up, so the check also ran on 6.48.3's assemblies: the same names (`Wolverine.dll` exports 745 types there), and none in JasperFx 2.81.0, JasperFx.Events 2.81.0 or Weasel.Core, Weasel.Postgresql and Weasel.EntityFrameworkCore 9.41.0, which those packages bring.

The exported-type diff, `diff -r` of the lists of `f836e9b` and of the change (exported types only):

```text
PouryaKit.Cqrs:                  + PouryaKit.Cqrs.OperationErrors
                                 + PouryaKit.Cqrs.PageErrorCodes
PouryaKit.Domain:                - PouryaKit.Domain.TypedIdJsonConverterFactory
                                 - PouryaKit.Domain.ValueObject
PouryaKit.Messaging.Wolverine:   - PouryaKit.Messaging.Wolverine.INotificationOutbox
                                 + PouryaKit.Messaging.Wolverine.IOutbox
PouryaKit.Persistence.EFCore:    - PouryaKit.Persistence.EFCore.EfUnitOfWorkExtensions
                                 - PouryaKit.Persistence.EFCore.EfUnitOfWorkExtensions+<G>$ACC19762D8E45BA19D63B8E8B96F05E4
                                 - PouryaKit.Persistence.EFCore.EfUnitOfWorkExtensions+<G>$ACC19762D8E45BA19D63B8E8B96F05E4+<M>$04F93354FF1C35BE716DE66BFAD4A08D
                                 + PouryaKit.Persistence.EFCore.EfUnitOfWorkDbContext
PouryaKit.Security:              - PouryaKit.Security.PasswordHashingOptions
                                 + PouryaKit.Security.PouryaKitPasswordHashingOptions
PouryaKit.Validation:            + PouryaKit.Validation.ValidationErrorCodes
```

The other fourteen assemblies export the same names. Among all type definitions, the change also adds the internal `Pbkdf2PasswordHasher+Validation`, makes `TypedIdJsonConverterFactory` internal, and renumbers or adds compiler-generated closure and state-machine types in `Dispatcher`, `Operation`, `OperationTypes`, `PasswordHashingServiceCollectionExtensions` and `FluentValidationStep`.

`python3 scripts/packaging/validate.py --no-fetch --since f836e9b` on the ticket's source (commit `1c8889c`, before this paragraph was added) printed `PASS 21 packages, 24 clean consumers (2 generated from templates, smoke tests passed: template-monolith (-n Acme.Shop) 4, template-modular-monolith (-n acme-shop) 5), negative controls and MinVer proof.` at version `0.1.0-preview.0.34`, the version its manifest records for that commit. It ran in full: the changed Kit packages are packed, so `--since` did not skip, and the Security clean consumer, which now configures `PouryaKitPasswordHashingOptions` through `Configure<T>` and resolves the hasher from a provider, restored and built against the packed packages.

## Pre-Wave 3 proofs before fixes, ticket #82

Ticket #82 of spec #74 (findings T1, T2, T3 and P7): four open questions about runtime guarantees, each settled by a run before any fix ticket starts, so that a red result cannot change a fix ticket's scope midway. A red result counts as a confirmed problem only when its failing output repeats on a second run; none is fixed here. Environment of every run: macOS (Darwin 27.0.0, arm64), .NET SDK 10.0.401, Docker 29.8.1, `postgres:18.6` through Testcontainers, WolverineFx 6.48.3, `Microsoft.Extensions.Caching.Hybrid` 10.10.0, EF Core 10.0.12, Npgsql 10.0.3.

| Question | Proven by | Result | Red once, or the two red runs |
|---|---|---|---|
| T1, a runtime guarantee: does a tag eviction hold for a cached query whose handler read before the command committed and returns after the eviction? | A PostgreSQL test of `CachingTests`' shape, not kept in the tree: Catalog's `GetProduct` for the Actor ada, held after its Dapper read by a test probe of `IReadConnectionFactory` whose connection's disposal waits for the test, so the handler has read and not returned; `RenameProduct` then commits and evicts `product:{id}`; the hold is released and ada reads again | **Red.** The rename succeeds and the held read answers the old name, as it should; the read after it answers the old name too, from an entry stored after the eviction, which lives until it expires (5 minutes). `HybridCache` stamps an entry when its run starts and, when one of the entry's tags was evicted while the factory ran, stamps it again when the factory completes, so the eviction does not reach it: decompiled `Microsoft.Extensions.Caching.Hybrid` 10.10.0, `DefaultHybridCache.StampedeState<TState, T>.BackgroundFetchAsync`, `if (!Cache.IsValid(CacheItem))` followed by `CacheItem.UnsafeSetCreationTimestamp(...)` with the time the factory returned. The Kit's skill already states this as a limit ([caching.md](../.agents/skills/pourya-kit/references/caching.md#limits)); the README's "evicted once an Operation commits" does not. The orchestrator sizes the fix | Both runs failed at the last read, on 2026-10-09 at 22:49:50 and 22:50:06 UTC: `Assert.Equal() Failure: Strings differ`, `Expected: "Desk lamp"`, `Actual:   "Lamp"`. The test is a correct test of the README's claim, not of what the Kit's skill records, so it stays out of the tree with its probe |
| T2: does a recurring command's cadence survive a release that changes its command's shape while an occurrence waits in the store? | `RecurringCommandTests.An_occurrence_stored_before_its_command_changed_shape_is_set_aside_and_logged_and_its_cadence_waits_for_the_next_host_start` | **The Host reports it; the cadence does not go on.** Inventory's `ReconcileStock`, hourly, on a database of its own: a Host stores the first occurrence and stops; the occurrence's stored body is rewritten through Wolverine's `EnvelopeSerializer` with the message an earlier shape wrote, its occurrence under a member since renamed; a restarted Host finds it waiting and stores none, and it is then made due. Wolverine cannot read it and sets it aside before the Kit's delivery runs (`HandlerPipeline.TryDeserializeEnvelope`, then `MoveToErrorQueue`): a dead letter at `local://inventory.reconcile-stock/` with `System.Text.Json.JsonException`, "JSON deserialization for type 'Inventory.StockItems.Features.ReconcileStock.ReconcileStockCommand' was missing required properties including: 'occurrence'.", and an Error entry of `Wolverine.Runtime.WolverineRuntime`, "Envelope Envelope #… from PouryaKit.Messaging.Wolverine to local://inventory.reconcile-stock/ was moved to the error queue", with that exception. No Receipt, no reconciliation, and no next occurrence until the next Host start, which stores the first one after now. That is not the guarantee recorded for a dead-lettered occurrence (the Wave 2 final review's package C, finding R2: the cadence goes on), which covers a failure of the occurrence's handler: an occurrence that no longer reads never reaches `DelayedCommandDelivery`. The orchestrator sizes the fix | Red once without `RespectRequiredConstructorParameters` in `UsePouryaKitWireJson`: the old body read with a default occurrence and ran, and the test failed waiting, `Not reached within 15 s: exists (select from wolverine.wolverine_dead_letters where received_at = 'local://inventory.reconcile-stock/')` |
| T3: does the Kit still need `UseEntityFrameworkCoreTransactions(TransactionMiddlewareMode.Eager)` in `UsePouryaKitWolverine(connectionName)`? | The whole suite with the line removed: `dotnet test PouryaKit.slnx` at `4bf4b82`, before any other change of the ticket | **No.** 830 passed, 0 failed, the suite's whole count, in 2m27s; `dotnet build PouryaKit.slnx` 0 warnings. The line and its two `using` directives are removed. What it registered, and why the Outbox and the Kit's pre-built handlers use none of it, is [ADR 0004](adr/0004-commit-through-wolverine-ef-outbox.md)'s #82 amendment | A removal: the passing suite without it is the proof |
| P7: is a delayed command or a recurring occurrence due later than `InboxStaleTime` handed out again, and with what effect? | `RecurringCommandTests.A_delayed_command_and_an_occurrence_due_after_the_stale_time_and_handed_out_again_while_they_run_apply_one_effect_each_and_store_one_next_occurrence` | **Handed out again only when a recovery poll comes while an attempt runs; one effect each, and one next occurrence.** On a database of its own, with a 500 ms stale time: the Stub's `WriteKindChangedNoteCommand` declared as the delayed command `stub.stale-note`, due 2 s after its commit, and `ReconcileStock` every 2 s, the held occurrence being one an occurrence's commit stored, so due a cadence later. Each is held after its Receipt until a copy a recovery poll handed out waits on that Receipt (a `Lock` wait in `pg_stat_activity`), then released: one note and one Receipt for the delayed command, one reconciliation and one Receipt for the occurrence, each delivered at least twice, one occurrence waiting next, no dead letter. A delivery waiting in the store is owned by no node, and the release takes only owned rows (`BumpStaleIncomingEnvelopesOperation`, `owner_id != 0 and timestamp <= cutoff`, decompiled `Wolverine.RDBMS` 6.48.3). In three runs with a temporary dump, not kept, each of the 7 occurrences not held, due 1 to 2 s after it was stored, had one attempt, and each held one had two | Red once with the delivery ignoring a Receipt already recorded (`RunDelayedCommandHandler`): `Assert.Equal() Failure: Collections differ`, `Expected: [[1, 1, 1, 1]]`, `Actual:   [[2, 1, 2, 1]]`, two notes and two reconciliations |
| P7's sentence | `PouryaKitMessageStoreOptions.InboxStaleTime`'s summary no longer tells every Host to set it longer than every retry delay plus every attempt's run, which an hourly occurrence never meets under the 5-minute default. It now reads: "longer than every retry delay together plus every attempt's run, queue wait included (`PouryaKitMessagingOptions.RetryDelays`), it keeps a delivery sent at its commit from going stale, but a delayed command or a recurring occurrence due later than it, as an hourly one always is, runs stale from its first attempt and, held by no instance while it waits, is handed out again only when a recovery poll finds an attempt of it still running"; the summary's last sentence adds that the Receipt keeps a recurring command's next occurrence to one | | |
| Docs | The Kit's skill, `messaging.md` ("The stale-time rule", and under "Recurring commands" what T2 showed); [`docs/architecture/README.md`](architecture/README.md) (the message store's registration, no EF Core middleware mode, and the stale time); ADR 0004 (#82 amendment) and ADR 0006 (a dated note on the #44 amendment, whose "No test pins this" the P7 test now does) | | |
| Out of scope | | Any fix of T1 or T2, which the orchestrator sizes as their own work; the README's caching line, which T1 questions and no change of this ticket alters; a test that an unheld attempt never overlaps a recovery poll, which no setting can make certain | |

`python3 scripts/packaging/validate.py --no-fetch --since f836e9b` on the ticket's source (commit `eb15280`, before this paragraph was added) printed `PASS 21 packages, 24 clean consumers (2 generated from templates, smoke tests passed: template-monolith (-n Acme.Shop) 4, template-modular-monolith (-n acme-shop) 5), negative controls and MinVer proof.` at version `0.1.0-preview.0.35`, the version its manifest records for that commit, with the Release runtime suite at 832 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped. It ran in full: 4 of the 13 changed paths can reach a package, `src/PouryaKit.Messaging.Wolverine.EFCore/PouryaKitMessageStoreOptions.cs` among them.

After the review (two lows, both XML summaries that restated the replaced sentences: `AddPouryaKitRecurringCommand`'s "the cadence goes on" and `RetryDelays`' stale-time advice), the orchestrator's fix commit `214597a` reran the same command and printed the same PASS line at `0.1.0-preview.0.37`, with the Release runtime suite at 832 passed, 0 failed.

## Pre-Wave 3 option validation at start, ticket #83

Ticket #83 of spec #74 (finding Q1 and its implementer constraint): the four Kit option types registered with a bare `AddOptions<T>()` are validated at start, each with `Validate` and `ValidateOnStart()` chained at its registration, as `AddPouryaKitJwtBearer` validates the same two claims. An intentional behaviour change: a Host given one of the refused values no longer starts, and the `OptionsValidationException` names the option; before #83 it started and failed at the first request, or never visibly. Environment of every run: macOS (Darwin 27.0.0, arm64), .NET SDK 10.0.401, Docker 29.8.1, `postgres:18.6` through Testcontainers, WolverineFx 6.48.3.

| Scenario | Proven by | Red once |
|---|---|---|
| `PouryaKitWebOptions`: an `ActorClaim` that is null, empty or whitespace, or a `TenantClaim` that is empty or whitespace, stops the Host at `StartAsync` with the messages of the same two checks on `PouryaKitJwtBearerOptions`, naming `PouryaKitWebOptions`. Observed at start: the test's Host maps no Module endpoints; one that does fails earlier, in `MapPouryaKitModuleEndpoints`, the first read | `MisconfiguredHostTests.A_blank_actor_or_tenant_claim_stops_the_host_at_start_naming_the_option`, five cases | With the Actor claim check removed, its three cases went red; with the tenant claim check removed, its two; with only `ValidateOnStart()` removed, all five |
| `PouryaKitTrustedPeerOptions`: a blank peer, empty or spaces, bound from configuration beside a valid one as the Hosts bind their peers, stops the Host at `StartAsync`. The type has no Kit registration, so its validation and `ValidateOnStart()` are in `AddPouryaKitWeb`, through `AddOptions<T>()`, which leaves the Hosts' `Configure<T>` binding as it was | `MisconfiguredHostTests.A_blank_trusted_peer_stops_the_host_at_start_naming_the_option`, two cases; `TrustedPeerTests`, the whole class, for the binding | With the check removed, both went red; with only its `ValidateOnStart()` removed, both |
| A null `TenantClaim` (read no tenant) and an empty peer list stay valid | `MisconfiguredHostTests.A_null_tenant_claim_and_an_empty_peer_list_let_the_host_start` | Red with the tenant check refusing null, and with the peer check refusing an empty list (that variant also turned eight other tests of the class red, whose Hosts configure no peer) |
| `PouryaKitMessagingOptions`: a null `RetryDelays`, or one holding a negative delay, stops the Host at `StartAsync`. Observed at the first read: Wolverine's options, built while `StartAsync` resolves the hosted services, read the delays before the start-time validation runs | `MisconfiguredHostTests.A_null_empty_or_negative_retry_schedule_stops_the_host_at_start_naming_the_option`, three cases; since ticket #95 (2026-10-10) four, the empty schedule added and the theory renamed to say so ([its section](#pre-wave-3-an-empty-retry-schedule-refused-at-start-ticket-95)) | With the null check removed, the null case went red; with the negative check removed, both negative cases. With only `ValidateOnStart()` removed, all three still passed: the first read fails the start before it, so the validation is the switch that proves the guarantee here, and `ValidateOnStart()` keeps it if that read ever moves later |
| A zero-delay schedule stays valid | `MisconfiguredHostTests.A_zero_delay_retry_schedule_lets_the_host_start` | Red with the check refusing a zero delay |
| `PouryaKitMessageStoreOptions`: an `InboxStaleTime` of zero or less stops the sample's Host at `StartAsync`, at the first read, when Wolverine's options are built, before the store is reached | `MisconfiguredHostTests.An_inbox_stale_time_of_zero_or_less_stops_the_host_at_start_naming_the_option`, two cases | With the check removed, both went red; with only `ValidateOnStart()` removed, both still passed, as for the retry schedule |
| Deviation: the ticket names "an empty or zero-delay retry schedule" as a positive case, but an empty schedule was never valid. On the unmodified tree it fails `StartAsync` with Wolverine's `InvalidOperationException`, "You must specify at least one delay time", thrown by `FailureActions.ScheduleRetry` when `src/PouryaKit.Messaging.Wolverine/PouryaKitWolverineExtension.cs` passes it the delays. The session decided on 2026-10-10: the validation refuses only null and negative delays, the positive case is the zero delay, and the option's docs say an empty schedule fails at start | The positive test with an empty schedule, run once on the unmodified tree and then removed | Open for the owner: an empty schedule meaning "no retry, set aside at once" in the Wolverine extension, or refused by the Kit's validation with its own message |
| Docs | The XML summaries of the four option types; the Kit's skill, `web.md` (the claims, the trusted peers) and `messaging.md` (the retries, the message store); [`docs/architecture/README.md`](architecture/README.md) (one bullet each in Web, Messaging.Wolverine and Messaging.Wolverine.EFCore) | |

`python3 scripts/packaging/validate.py --no-fetch --since ff60843` on the ticket's source (commit `d033e8d`, before this paragraph was added) printed `PASS 21 packages, 24 clean consumers (2 generated from templates, smoke tests passed: template-monolith (-n Acme.Shop) 4, template-modular-monolith (-n acme-shop) 5), negative controls and MinVer proof.` at version `0.1.0-preview.0.37`, the version its manifest records for that commit, with the Release runtime suite at 853 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped. It ran in full: the three changed registrations and four option types are in packed packages, and the sample's Host and both generated Hosts, whose option values are all valid, still start.

After the review (two lows: the `RetryDelays` summary and the skill overclaimed that an empty schedule fails naming the option, and `AddPouryaKitWeb`'s summary omitted its two validations), the orchestrator's fix commit `1e3f100` reran the same command and printed the same PASS line at `0.1.0-preview.0.39`.

## Pre-Wave 3 Cqrs, Dapper and Observability fixes, ticket #84

Ticket #84 of spec #74 (findings V1, V2, V8, Q8 and Q9's Cqrs and Dapper sites): the dispatch path's telemetry, its allocation when nothing listens and two lookups, fixed where the final assessment confirmed them, and V1 measured only. No public API changed, no package or version pin was added, and V1 changed no code.

| Scenario | Proven by | Red once |
|---|---|---|
| V2: `pouryakit.operation.duration` declares ASP.NET Core's second-scale bucket boundaries for `http.server.request.duration` (`dotnet/aspnetcore@7387de9`, tag `v10.0.0`, `MetricsConstants.ShortSecondsBucketBoundaries`) as its `InstrumentAdvice<double>`, and the OpenTelemetry SDK exports them with no view | `HostRecipeTests.Inventory_hosts_deliveries_reach_the_exporters_of_its_service_defaults`, which gained one assertion: every exported point of the histogram has the fourteen boundaries, 0.005 to 10, read from `MetricPoint.GetHistogramBuckets()` | Red without the advice: `Assert.All() Failure: 6 out of 6 items in the collection did not pass`, `Actual: List<double> [0, 5, 10, 25, 50, ···]`, the SDK's millisecond defaults |
| V8: the dispatcher's log scope carries the Operation's `OperationId`, `CorrelationId` and `Module` beside its type name, so its logs join their request without a tracing exporter | `HostRecipeTests.With_serilog_the_kits_logs_reach_a_sink_the_host_registers`, which gained one assertion: an entry the `Dispatcher` logged in `Inventory.Host` carries `Deliver`, the delivery's own `OperationId` (from `DeliveryProbes.ContextsOf`), the correlation of the `CreateProduct` it continues and `InventoryModule`. Serilog's logger provider turns the scope's values into the entry's properties, so the existing sink is the capture | Red with the scope given nulls for the three values: `Assert.Contains() Failure: Item not found in collection`, every tuple `00000000-…, 00000000-…, null` |
| Q8: the fast path keeps the two-part predicate: it is taken only with no activity listener and the histogram disabled, so a Host with either listener alone still observes | `ObservabilityTests.With_only_a_meter_listener_a_command_still_records_its_duration`, `ObservabilityTests.With_only_an_activity_listener_a_command_is_still_an_activity`; `RecordedTelemetry` can leave out either listener | Red with `\|\|` turned into `&&`: both new cases failed (`Filter not matched in collection`), the class's other five passed |
| Q8: with nothing listening, the wrapper returns the inner dispatcher's own task and allocates nothing: its delegates are static, so no closure, delegate or state machine is created before the check | `ObservedDispatcherTests.With_nothing_listening_the_observed_dispatcher_returns_the_dispatchers_own_task_and_allocates_nothing` (`PouryaKit.Cqrs.Tests`, which now references `PouryaKit.Observability`): a scope's observed dispatcher around a stand-in that returns completed tasks, warmed with 1,000 rounds, then `GC.GetAllocatedBytesForCurrentThread()` around 10,000 rounds of a command, a command with a response and a query | Red on the code before the change: `Actual: Tuple (False, False, False, 15840000)` in Debug and `(False, False, False, 5280000)` in Release, 528 and 176 bytes per dispatch; after it 0 in both, with the inner task returned as it is |
| Q9: `PageSql.DbTypes` is a `FrozenDictionary`, as `PageDeclaration`'s and `FilterField`'s lookups are; `FilterOperatorNames.TryParseName` reads one cached `Enum.GetValues` array and keeps its loop, so a null name still answers false | `PageTests.Each_operator_has_one_short_name_that_parses_back_to_it`, which gained the null case; `DapperTests` and `PagingTests` through the full run | The null case is red with `ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull(name)` added to `TryParseName`; the frozen lookup answers as before, a refactor with no red run |
| The Kit builds and the ticket's suites pass | `dotnet build PouryaKit.slnx`: 0 warnings, 0 errors. `ModularMonolith.Tests` 571 passed, 0 failed; `PouryaKit.Cqrs.Tests` 46; `PouryaKit.ArchitectureTests` 102; `check-docs.py` and `check-ai-setup.py` 0 failures | |
| Docs | The Kit's skill (`observability.md`: the one check, either listener alone, the histogram's boundaries, the log scope; `commands-queries-validation.md`: the logging step's scope), [`docs/architecture/README.md`](architecture/README.md) (the pipeline's logging step, the wrapper's fast path and the histogram's advice), `README.md`'s observability line, and the provenance record of `PouryaKit.Observability` (the boundaries' source, adoption `pattern`: the values only) | |
| Out of scope | | No change from V1's numbers (below). Q9's other two sites, `Subscriptions.ReservedQueues` and the header-value check, belong to other tickets of the spec |

### V1: the measurement

`EnterQuery` and `EnterChecks` each open a reading boundary, and each captures every tracked entry (`src/PouryaKit.Cqrs/Pipeline/Dispatcher.cs`, `src/PouryaKit.Persistence.EFCore/UnitOfWork/TrackedState.cs`). Both stay (ADR 0003): this ticket only sizes them. A throwaway console project outside the repository, built in Release against this branch, measured two things:

- **The second capture per nested query.** A command handler attaches N entities of five scalar properties to a DbContext that never opens a connection, then times 100 nested queries whose handler returns at once. The guard is `EfUnitOfWork`'s (`src/PouryaKit.Persistence.EFCore/UnitOfWork/EfUnitOfWork.cs`, `TrackedState.Capture(dbContext, includeQueryLoads: true)` compared when the boundary closes); a switch leaves out the checks boundary's capture, which is what removing it would save. 41 alternating rounds of each shape, medians.
- **The DbContext a Dapper-only read constructs.** In the Proving Sample's Host, built and migrated against `postgres:18.6` but not started, Catalog's `ListProducts` through the dispatcher (an empty first Page: its select and count), 1,001 reads; and 15 rounds of 2,000 scopes of Catalog's Operation, timed and counted with `GC.GetAllocatedBytesForCurrentThread()` with nothing more, with its `IReadingStateGuard` resolved, and with its two boundaries opened and closed.

```csharp
// The guard: EfUnitOfWork's, or with the checks boundary's capture left out.
public IDisposable OnlyReading(string step) =>
    SkipChecksCapture && step == "An authorizer or a validator"
        ? NoCapture.Instance
        : new ReadingState(TrackedState.Capture(db, includeQueryLoads: true), step);

// The command's handler: N tracked entities, then 100 timed nested queries.
db.Items.AttachRange(Item.Many(command.Entities));
var started = Stopwatch.GetTimestamp();
for (var i = 0; i < command.Queries; i++)
{
    await dispatcher.QueryAsync(new NothingQuery(), cancellationToken);
}

return Stopwatch.GetElapsedTime(started).Ticks;

// A Dapper-only read's guard, per scope: step 0 opens the scope only.
using var scope = host.Services.CreateScope();
scope.ServiceProvider.BeginOperation(context, typeof(CatalogModule));
var guard = scope.ServiceProvider.GetRequiredService<IReadingStateGuard>(); // step 1
guard.OnlyReading("A query").Dispose(); // step 2
guard.OnlyReading("An authorizer or a validator").Dispose();
```

| N tracked entities | One boundary, capture and comparison | A nested query with both captures | With the outer capture only | The second capture | Its share |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 0.3 to 0.9 µs | 1.2 to 2.9 µs | 0.9 to 1.9 µs | 0.2 to 1.0 µs | 19 to 35 % |
| 10 | 8.3 to 9.1 µs | 18.5 to 24.7 µs | 8.5 to 13.4 µs | 10.0 to 11.3 µs | 46 to 54 % |
| 100 | 78 to 79 µs | 162 to 171 µs | 81 to 84 µs | 81 to 86 µs | 50 to 51 % |
| 1000 | 1,087 to 1,162 µs | 2,060 to 2,073 µs | 893 to 905 µs | 1,155 to 1,180 µs | 56 to 57 % |

A Dapper-only read: the read took 877 to 1,194 µs (median); its guard added 21.0 to 21.6 µs and 59,300 to 59,450 bytes per read, 1.8 to 2.4 % of the read: resolving it, which constructs Catalog's DbContext and `EfUnitOfWork`, 20.6 to 21.6 µs and 58,140 bytes, and its two captures of the empty change tracker 0.07 to 0.46 µs and about 1,200 bytes.

The ranges are two runs on 2026-10-10: macOS 27.0.1 (Darwin 27.0.0), Apple silicon, 18 cores, .NET 10.0.12 (SDK 10.0.401), Docker 29.8.1. Other agents' test suites ran beside them (a load average of 6 to 8; in the first run three `postgres:18.6`, a `rabbitmq:4.3.6` and a `redis:8.10.1` container of another suite), so the absolute times are indicative; with 10 or more tracked entities the shares agree between the runs. What they say: the second capture costs about 1 µs per tracked entity per nested query, half of the query's own cost once anything is tracked, and a Dapper-only read pays about 21 µs and 58 KB to construct a DbContext it never uses. Neither number changes code here; the orchestrator sizes any change, and none may remove a comparison.

`python3 scripts/packaging/validate.py --no-fetch --since ff60843` on the ticket's source (commit `d9d57da`, before this paragraph was added) printed `PASS 21 packages, 24 clean consumers (2 generated from templates, smoke tests passed: template-monolith (-n Acme.Shop) 4, template-modular-monolith (-n acme-shop) 5), negative controls and MinVer proof.` at version `0.1.0-preview.0.38`, the version its manifest records for that commit, with the Release runtime suite at 842 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped. It ran in full: 10 of the 22 changed paths can reach a package, `src/PouryaKit.Cqrs/Paging/FilterOperatorNames.cs` among them. Its first run on the same commit failed in that suite at 841 passed, 1 failed: `RecurringCommandTests.An_occurrence_stored_before_its_command_changed_shape_is_set_aside_and_logged_and_its_cadence_waits_for_the_next_host_start` could not start its Host, `53300: sorry, too many clients already` from the test container's PostgreSQL, while other agents' suites ran on the same Docker host; the rerun, with nothing changed, passed.

## Pre-Wave 3 Messaging and Persistence fixes, ticket #85

Ticket #85 of spec #74 (findings P1, Q5, V10, V11, V12, Q13, Q9 and D15): one high, a published Snapshot that could differ from the row its Operation committed, against ADR 0003, and seven smaller fixes of the delivery and commit paths. No public member changed, no package or version pin was added. `src/PouryaKit.Messaging/ISnapshotMapper.cs` was added to the brief's files for P1's XML summary by the session's decision of 2026-10-10. Every proof runs against PostgreSQL in Testcontainers, `CrossProcessTracingTests` against RabbitMQ too.

| Scenario | Proven by | Red once |
|---|---|---|
| P1: a commit that changes an aggregate with a declared Snapshot is refused, naming the property, when the database generates a value of the aggregate itself: the Stub's `Ticket`, whose `Serial` has a default value SQL; nothing commits and nothing is published | `SnapshotTests.A_snapshot_of_an_aggregate_whose_database_generates_a_value_it_maps_fails_the_operation_that_saves_it` | Red with the refusal off: `Expected: ["printed by the database"]`, `Actual: [null]`, the row committed the database's serial and the Snapshot published none |
| P1: the same when the value is in a part the aggregate owns and the mapper reads: the Stub's `Drawer`, whose owned `Knob.Label` is a computed column, so the refusal covers the aggregate's mapped graph (the aggregate, the types it owns, the children it holds through a navigation, their derived types), not its root alone | `SnapshotTests.A_snapshot_of_an_aggregate_whose_database_generates_a_value_of_an_owned_part_it_maps_fails_the_operation_that_saves_it` | Red with the refusal off: `Expected: ["BRASS"]`, `Actual: [null]`. Red alone, the root's test green, with the walk kept to the root |
| P1, review finding F2: a value a client-side value generator sets for good when the entity is added is not refused, since the database writes none of it: the Stub's `Badge`, whose `Code` is generated on add by EF's `GuidValueGenerator`, commits and its Snapshot carries the code committed. A non-key property generated on add only is refused unless its mapping names a generator, the one EF's `IValueGeneratorSelector` returns, whose values are not temporary | `SnapshotTests.A_snapshot_of_an_aggregate_whose_value_a_client_side_generator_sets_carries_the_value_committed` | Red with that exception taken out: `System.InvalidOperationException : A Snapshot of Badge is declared, but Badge.Code is generated by the database when it is saved`. Both regressions above went red again with the refusal off after the fix, and the owned-part one alone with the walk kept to the root |
| P1: the existing Snapshot tests stay green; the Kit's own conventions (Guid version 7 keys, Kit-set version and audit fields, a TPH discriminator) meet no refusal | `SnapshotTests`: 33 passed | |
| V11: the key-space check no longer walks the EF model at each commit: the model's aggregate types and their roots are read once per DbContext type, in a static cache as `SnapshotMappers.Mappings` is; the throw stays | `SnapshotTests.A_snapshot_declared_for_aggregates_of_two_mapped_hierarchies_fails_the_operation_that_would_publish_it` | By design, not a red run: the verdict itself is not cached. That test's Host declares one Snapshot more over the same Module and DbContext as the fixture Host, and a Host's declarations are its own, so a verdict per DbContext type and Module shared across Hosts would skip it; the check runs at each commit over the cached read |
| Q5: Messaging reads the Actor kind header with Web's name switch: exactly `Person` or `System`, anything else the Kit's `InvalidOperationException` | `OperationContextTests.An_actor_kind_header_that_is_not_exactly_person_or_system_is_refused` (`"1"`, `"Person, System"`, `"Robot"`, `" Person"`, `"System "`); `OperationContextTests.A_person_and_a_system_actor_continue_as_themselves`; `OperationContextTests.An_undefined_numeric_actor_kind_cannot_continue_as_a_system_actor` unchanged in what it asserts | Red with the header parsed as an enum again: four cases accepted (`No exception was thrown`), and `"Robot"` threw `System.ArgumentException` |
| V10: a delivery's Operation activity carries `pouryakit.subscription`, the subscription's name, set by `DeliverHandler`; the duration histogram keeps its tags | `CrossProcessTracingTests.A_product_created_in_the_catalog_host_and_stocked_by_inventory_host_is_one_trace_with_the_upstream_tracestate`, both cases: each attempt of `inventory.product-created` and the delivery of `catalog.product-created.publish-product-created` | Red with the tag removed: `Expected: ("InventoryModule", "inventory.product-created", …)`, `Actual: ("InventoryModule", null, …)`, in both cases |
| V10: a delayed or recurring command's run carries its declared name the same way, set by `RunDelayedCommandHandler` | `DelayedCommandTests.A_delayed_commands_run_is_an_operation_tagged_with_its_name` | Red with the tag removed: `Expected: stub.delayed-note`, `Actual: null` |
| Q9: `Subscriptions.ReservedQueues` is a frozen set that keeps its ordinal-ignore-case comparer | `DelayedCommandTests.A_delayed_command_named_after_a_queue_wolverine_keeps_for_itself_is_refused` (`scheduled`, and the mixed-case `ScheDuled`) | Red with `StringComparer.Ordinal`: `ScheDuled` was accepted |
| V12: the idempotency key cleanup cleans the DbContexts `AddPouryaKitEfCore` recorded as `DbContextConnection`, not EF's non-generic options registrations | `IdempotencyRetentionTests`: 9 passed | A change of lookup: the existing tests are the proof |
| Q13: the rollback's `CancellationToken.None` states why: a rollback completes whatever the caller cancelled | The comment in `EfUnitOfWork.RunAsync` | |
| D15: the idempotency store, its cleanup and its table are PostgreSQL-only, as the Receipts are; `AddPouryaKitEfCore` installs the cleanup in every Host, and it walks every Unit of Work DbContext, probing each with `to_regclass` and returning silently for a Module with no `[Idempotent]` command | The XML docs of `AddPouryaKitEfCore`, `CreateIdempotencyTable` and `PouryaKitIdempotencyOptions`; `persistence.md`; one line in the plan's section C and one in [`docs/architecture/README.md`](architecture/README.md) | No code change |

## Pre-Wave 3 the Web and shared JSON fixes, ticket #86

Ticket #86 of spec #74 (findings P8, V9 under the owner's decision D9, V14 and Q9's Web site): three fixes at the HTTP and wire trust boundary and one idiom. V9 and V14 are intentional behaviour changes. No public API changed: the one new type, `KitBadRequestException`, is internal to `PouryaKit.Web`. No package or version pin was added. The XML summary of `UsePouryaKitWolverine` in `src/PouryaKit.Messaging.Wolverine/WolverineHostApplicationBuilderExtensions.cs`, outside the brief's files, was changed by one sentence on the session's grant of 2026-10-10, because it restates the wire's reading rule.

| Scenario | Proven by | Red once |
|---|---|---|
| P8: a capitalised `Filter[...]` key filters the Page, as `page`, `size`, `sort` and `direction` are already read ignoring case through `IQueryCollection` | `PagingTests.A_filter_key_whose_prefix_is_capitalised_filters_the_page` | Red with the prefix compared `Ordinal`: the unfiltered Page came back, `Collections differ at index 1`, `Expected: "Floor lamp"`, `Actual: "Oak desk"` |
| V9 over HTTP: a body that names a member twice, `name` twice or `name` and `Name`, is a body that cannot be read: 400 as a problem response, without `errors` and, being ASP.NET Core's own refusal, without `detail`, and nothing is saved | `WebTests.A_body_that_names_a_member_twice_answers_400_as_a_problem_response_and_nothing_is_saved` (two cases) | Red with `AllowDuplicateProperties` at its default: both cases answered 200 (`Expected: BadRequest`, `Actual: OK`), the body read with its last value |
| V9 on the wire: a broker envelope whose body names a member of its contract twice is dead-lettered at the receiving Host's queue with `Duplicate property 'name' encountered during deserialization of type 'ModularMonolith.Tests.ShapeV1'.`, and no subscription receives a copy | `StrictWireReadingTests.A_body_that_names_a_member_of_its_contract_twice_is_dead_lettered_at_the_host_queue_with_the_error_and_never_handled` (two Hosts on RabbitMQ; the publishing contract writes the name again from `[JsonExtensionData]`, which System.Text.Json writes without checking names) | The same mutation: the handler received the envelope (`Assert.DoesNotContain() Failure: Filter matched in collection`). One line covers both sides because `UsePouryaKitWireJson` applies `UsePouryaKitJson` first |
| V14: the Kit's own 400 reasons reach the caller as the problem's `detail`, word for word: a page query string (six cases), a missing or unreadable `Idempotency-Key` (three cases on POST, and on PUT and DELETE), a trusted peer's incomplete or unreadable context headers (five cases) | `PagingTests.A_page_query_string_that_cannot_be_read_answers_400_as_a_problem_response`; `IdempotencyTests.A_request_without_a_readable_idempotency_key_answers_400_as_a_problem_response_and_creates_nothing`; `IdempotencyTests.An_idempotent_put_or_delete_without_a_key_answers_400_and_with_one_replays_the_first_response`; `TrustedPeerTests.A_trusted_peer_whose_context_headers_are_incomplete_or_unreadable_is_refused_with_400` | Red with the refusing sites throwing ASP.NET Core's bare `BadHttpRequestException`, as before the subclass: all 16 cases failed, each with `KeyNotFoundException` at `GetProperty("detail")` |
| V14: a 400 ASP.NET Core raises itself, malformed JSON or a route value that does not parse, keeps no `detail`, in Development and Production | `WebTests.A_request_that_cannot_be_read_answers_400_as_a_problem_response_and_is_not_logged_as_an_error` (the existing four cases) | Red with the handler writing every `BadHttpRequestException`'s message: all four failed at the `detail` assertion (`Assert.False() Failure`) |
| Q9: the header-value check uses `ContainsAnyExceptInRange(' ', '~')`, as the password hasher's digit check does, instead of LINQ; the values it accepts are unchanged | `TrustedPeerTests` (a control character and a non-ASCII tenant refused), `IdempotencyTests` (an empty and a space-padded key refused), `OutgoingHttpTests.A_context_value_that_is_not_a_safe_header_value_fails_the_call_before_it_is_sent` | An idiom change, not a guarantee: no red run; the existing tests stay green |
| The Kit builds and every test passes | `dotnet build PouryaKit.slnx`: 0 warnings, 0 errors. `ModularMonolith.Tests` 573 passed, 0 failed (the `PagingTests`, `WebTests` and `StrictWireReadingTests` cases above are new); `PouryaKit.ArchitectureTests` 102 passed; `PouryaKit.Domain.Tests` 40 passed | |

## Pre-Wave 3 plan, roadmap and glossary, ticket #89

Ticket #89 of spec #74 (findings P3, P4, P5, P11, V6 and D5): the plan says what is built and what Wave 3's spec starts from, and the glossary keeps one meaning per word. Documentation only: no Kit code, package, test or template changed, and no section of the plan moved or was renamed. Each change to the plan is a dated note beside the text it corrects, which stays readable as history.

| What | Where | Evidence |
|---|---|---|

## Pre-Wave 3 an empty retry schedule refused at start, ticket #95

Ticket #95 of spec #74, the owner's answer of 2026-10-10 to the question #83's deviation row left open ([its section](#pre-wave-3-option-validation-at-start-ticket-83)): an empty `PouryaKitMessagingOptions.RetryDelays` is refused by the Kit's own validation at start, so every refused value of the option fails the same way, naming it. An intentional change of the failure, not of what is valid: a Host with an empty schedule failed at start before and still does, now with an `OptionsValidationException` naming the option instead of Wolverine's `InvalidOperationException`, "You must specify at least one delay time", which names no Kit setting; a zero delay stays valid. `PouryaKitWolverineExtension` is unchanged, so Wolverine's refusal stays as a backstop a validated Host never reaches. No public member, package or version pin changed. #83's deviation row stays as its record; only its scenario row's test pointer follows the renamed theory. Environment of every run: macOS (Darwin 27.0.0, arm64), .NET SDK 10.0.401, Docker 29.8.1, `postgres:18.6` through Testcontainers, WolverineFx 6.48.3.

| Scenario | Proven by | Red once |
|---|---|---|
| An empty `RetryDelays` stops the Host at `StartAsync` with an `OptionsValidationException` of `PouryaKitMessagingOptions` whose one failure is "PouryaKitMessagingOptions.RetryDelays is empty; set at least one delay, where a zero delay retries at once." The check is a third `Validate` in `UsePouryaKitWolverine`'s chain, after the null check and before the negative one, and lets null through, so a null schedule still fails with its own message alone | `MisconfiguredHostTests.A_null_empty_or_negative_retry_schedule_stops_the_host_at_start_naming_the_option`, four cases: null, empty and #83's two negative ones | With the empty check removed, the empty case alone went red: `Assert.Throws() Failure: Exception type was not an exact match`, expected `OptionsValidationException`, actual `InvalidOperationException`, "You must specify at least one delay time"; the other three cases and the zero-delay test stayed green |
| A zero-delay schedule stays valid | `MisconfiguredHostTests.A_zero_delay_retry_schedule_lets_the_host_start`, unchanged | Green with and without the new check |
| The Kit builds and the tests pass | `dotnet build PouryaKit.slnx`: 0 warnings, 0 errors. `MisconfiguredHostTests` 29 passed; `PouryaKit.ArchitectureTests` 102 passed. `ModularMonolith.Tests` 600 passed, 1 failed, in each of five runs, four before and one after the rebase onto `a73c2e1`: `RecurringCommandTests.An_occurrence_stored_before_its_command_changed_shape_is_set_aside_and_logged_and_its_cadence_waits_for_the_next_host_start`, on PostgreSQL's `53300: sorry, too many clients already` while its Hosts migrate the message store, the fourth run with no other suite's container on the machine; `RecurringCommandTests` alone passed 11 of 11. The test composes three Hosts on a database of its own against the fixture's container, whose connection limit is PostgreSQL's default; this ticket's change opens no connection. On the base `c3a3467`, checked out in the same worktree, the same command failed the same test the same way (599 passed, 1 failed), so the failure is the integration branch's, not this ticket's. The Release runtime suite of the validation below passed it | |
| Docs | The `RetryDelays` XML summary, the Kit's skill (`messaging.md`, the retries) and [`docs/architecture/README.md`](architecture/README.md) (the retry schedule's bullet, now of tickets #83 and #95) say that null, an empty schedule or a negative delay stops the Host at start naming the option, so a failed delivery is always retried at least once; the architecture bullet adds that Wolverine's `ScheduleRetry` refusal is a backstop a validated Host never reaches. A search for the phrase #83's three sentences used for Wolverine's refusal, across `src/`, the skill, `docs/architecture/` and this file, finds no line: #83's record never quoted it | |

## Pre-Wave 3 one message-store schema per Host, ticket #96

Ticket #96 of spec #74 (Wolverine issue #4923): instances of one Host share one store and one schema, and two different Hosts never share a schema. Documentation only, by the owner's decision of 2026-10-10: no runtime guard, check or test, and no code changed but one XML summary.

| What | Where | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| The rule, with the #4923 mechanism: on a schema two applications share, a node without the other's handlers recovers its deliveries and dead-letters them | The `Durability` summary of `PouryaKitMessageStoreOptions` (`src/PouryaKit.Messaging.Wolverine.EFCore/PouryaKitMessageStoreOptions.cs`); the Kit's skill, `messaging.md`, "The message store" (the `Durability` bullet and the schema bullet); [`docs/architecture/README.md`](architecture/README.md), the paragraph of `UsePouryaKitWolverine(connectionName, schema, configure)` | A search for Balanced's old promise of safety for instances on one store, over `src/`, the Kit's skill, `docs/architecture/` and this record, outside `bin/` and `obj/`, prints two lines, the summary's and the skill's, and each continues with "when all are instances of one Host". The architecture table row of `UsePouryaKitWolverine` lists types and restates nothing; `GLOSSARY.md`'s Outbox and Inbox already name a Module's share of its Host's store; the skill's `rabbitmq.md` keeps its broker rule, which concerns a queue |

## Pre-Wave 3 the OpenAPI options validated at start, ticket #97

Ticket #97 of spec #74, the fifth option type its problem statement counts: `PouryaKitOpenApiOptions` is validated when the Host starts. An intentional behaviour change: a Host whose sunsets hold one of the four refused values no longer starts, and the `OptionsValidationException` names each refused key. Before #97 a key that names no version threw an `InvalidOperationException` only when Asp.Versioning first read its options, as endpoints were built, at the first request or never, and the other three values were accepted without a word: two keys of one version merged into one policy, the last date winning and both links kept; a relative link was written with a leading `/`; an entry with neither value made an empty policy. No public member, package or version pin changed. `AddSunsets` is unchanged, so its throw stays as a backstop a validated Host never reaches; its parse and the validation's, both through `ApiVersionParser.Default`, are the one duplication the ticket accepts. The shape is a private nested `IValidateOptions<PouryaKitOpenApiOptions>`, registered with `TryAddEnumerable` beside `AddOptions<T>().ValidateOnStart()` as `ConfiguredCors` validates `PouryaKitCorsOptions`, in place of the ticket's literal "shape #83 used", a chain of `Validate(predicate, message)`: a chained message is fixed at registration and cannot name the key, and a sunset dictionary holds several entries (the session's decision of 2026-10-10). Environment of every run: macOS (Darwin 27.0.0, arm64), .NET SDK 10.0.401, Docker 29.8.1, `postgres:18.6` through Testcontainers, WolverineFx 6.48.3.

| Scenario | Proven by | Red once |
|---|---|---|
| A key that names no API version (`v1`), two keys that parse to one version (`2` and `2.0`), a `Link` that is not an absolute URI (`/api-v3-sunset`, which the binder reads as a relative `Uri`) and a sunset with neither a `Date` nor a `Link` (`4.0` with no value) each stop the Host at `StartAsync` with an `OptionsValidationException` of `PouryaKitOpenApiOptions`, whose failure line names `PouryaKitOpenApiOptions.Sunsets`, the key, the rule and the fix; two refused entries give a line each. The values are bound from configuration beside the sample's valid `1.0` sunset, as the Hosts bind them, and observed at start, by `ValidateOnStart()`: the test's Host maps no versioned endpoint, so nothing else reads them | `MisconfiguredHostTests.A_sunset_naming_no_version_or_a_version_twice_or_a_relative_link_or_nothing_stops_the_host_at_start_naming_its_key`, five cases | With the key check removed, the `v1` case and the two-entry case went red; with the duplicate check removed, the `2` and `2.0` case; with the absolute-URI check removed, the relative-link case and the two-entry case; with the empty-entry check removed, the `4.0` case; with only `ValidateOnStart()` removed, all five, whose Hosts then started. With the duplicates grouped by the raw key instead of the parsed version, the `2` and `2.0` case went red |
| The sample's own sunset, bound from its `appsettings.json` in the tests' output, and no sunset at all let the Host start, and the bound keys are `1.0` and none | `MisconfiguredHostTests.The_samples_sunset_or_none_lets_the_host_start`, two cases | Green under each of the six variants above |
| The sample's Host still reports its configured sunset | `OpenApiTests`, the whole class | |
| Refuted: the ticket's item 1 says `ValidateOnStart()` also makes a malformed `Date` fail at start, in the binder, naming the section, an inference of the scout's that no run had checked. In a Host, `Configure<T>(section)` uses the reflection binder, whose `BindDictionary` (`Microsoft.Extensions.Configuration.Binder` of the shared framework 10.0.12, decompiled) catches any exception while binding one entry and rethrows only when `BinderOptions.ErrorOnUnknownConfiguration` is set. So `PouryaKit:OpenApi:Sunsets:5.0:Date` set to `next June`, or a `Link` of `http://`, drops the whole `5.0` entry without an error: the version reports no sunset, and the validation never sees the entry. A first probe, a file-based app (`dotnet run probe.cs`), threw an `InvalidOperationException` naming the path, because such an app enables the source-generated binder, which throws; the same probe with `PublishAot` and `EnableConfigurationBindingGenerator` off dropped the entry, and threw with `ErrorOnUnknownConfiguration` set | A Host of the theory's shape, with the sample's configuration and `5.0:Date` set to `next June`, started, and its bound `Sunsets` held `1.0` alone; the fact planned for the binder's failure was dropped. The option's summary and the skill's `openapi.md` state the silent drop | Open for the owner: `ErrorOnUnknownConfiguration` where a Host binds is a composition policy over every option bound from configuration, not a sunset check; the session decided on 2026-10-10 that this ticket changes no Host |
| The Kit builds and the tests pass | `dotnet build PouryaKit.slnx`: 0 warnings, 0 errors. `MisconfiguredHostTests` 36 passed; `OpenApiTests` 14 passed; `ModularMonolith.Tests` 608 passed, 0 failed, in each of two runs, the known `53300` failure of [#95's section](#pre-wave-3-an-empty-retry-schedule-refused-at-start-ticket-95) occurring in neither; `PouryaKit.ArchitectureTests` 102 passed | |
| Docs | The XML summaries of `PouryaKitOpenApiOptions.Sunsets` and `AddPouryaKitOpenApi`; the Kit's skill, `openapi.md` ("Deprecation and sunset", "Testing versions and documents"); [`docs/architecture/README.md`](architecture/README.md), a bullet in `PouryaKit.Web.OpenApi`. They say what is refused and that the Host does not start, and what stays accepted: a past date, since nothing reads the clock, and a version no Module declares, known only once the endpoints are mapped. A search for the skill's old sentence on a key that names no version, across `src/`, the skill, `docs/architecture/` and this file, outside `bin/` and `obj/`, prints nothing | |
